The content of SKILL.md is:
# Orchestrated Batch Workflow
You are the orchestrator for this batch. You work in the main session and **delegate each phase to the matching subagent** via the `agent` tool. Subagents cannot delegate further, so all routing decisions happen here.
Repo rules live in `AGENTS.md` — every phase must respect them. No agent or step may run build, compile, Gradle, KSP, Hilt, Room validation, lint, unit tests, Android tests, or IDE sync unless the human explicitly approves a focused command. Suggest validation commands; do not execute them uninvited.
## Scope rules
Pipeline-local work only unless the approved plan says otherwise.
Allowed: files directly used by the batch, shared infrastructure the batch depends on, tests/golden/architecture/migration tests the batch needs, docs describing changed contracts.
Forbidden: broad unrelated cleanup, unrelated areas, weakening architecture, bypassing lifecycle coordinators, removing tests to pass, adding `@Ignore`, swallowing errors to hide bugs, destructive migrations unless explicitly approved.
## Default batch loop
```text
scout (if needed)
→ coder or specialist-coder
→ tester-runtime or tester-static
→ reviewer-strict
```
For risky work (workers, privacy/security, Room/migrations, lifecycle paths, architecture guards, backup/export/cloud AI, cross-layer changes):
```text
scout verifies current source
→ relevant guardian (architecture-guardian, privacy-security-guardian, room-migration-guardian)
→ specialist-coder implements minimal diff
→ tester-runtime adds targeted tests
→ guardian re-check if needed
→ reviewer-strict reviews final diff
```
## Fix-review loop
Do not stop after coding. The reviewer must validate deeply. If the verdict is FAIL, route issues back to the coder, fix, and re-review. Cap at 2 review iterations and 2 debug iterations (`workflows.json` limits), then stop and report BLOCKED. Only finish when the reviewer verdict is PASS or the human explicitly stops the loop.
## Hard stops
Stop and report if: no approved plan exists (route to planning first), the plan does not match current code, named files/classes are missing, reviewer returns FAIL past the iteration cap, tests fail with no obvious root cause, an unexpected schema migration appears, privacy/security behavior is ambiguous, a lifecycle legal path is unclear, scope grows beyond the requested batch, or destructive git/file commands would be needed.
## Review protocol
Reviewer findings must use the format `[ISSUE-N] [SEVERITY] problem - file - suggested fix` with verdict `PASS` or `FAIL`. Any issue means FAIL.
## Handoff
At the end, produce: summary of issues fixed, files changed, tests added/updated, docs updated, reviewer final verdict, known risks, commands for the human to run, expected failures if any, follow-up items.

1: # PR-GR-12 — Prove direct write-barrier dominance
2:
3: ## Agent mission
4:
5: Replace the current **lexical “barrier appears earlier in the callable”** rule with a conservative control-flow proof:
6:
7: > Every reachable path from a callable entry to a direct DB mutation must cross a canonical write-barrier check, or the mutation must be lexically inside a verified canonical guarded scope.
8:
9: This PR activates enforcement for policy entries whose barrier requirement is currently `direct`.
10:
11: It does **not** prove `helper` or `workerMediated` entries. Those remain outside active mediation enforcement until GR-13 through GR-15.
12:
13: ## Why this PR is necessary
14:
15: At the current anchor SHA, D4 treats a direct-mode mutation as guarded when the masked text before the call contains `writeBarrier.checkWritesAllowed(...)` or `writeBarrier.runWrite(...)`. That can accept a barrier in a different branch, an unrelated lambda, after a prior mutation, or in dead code. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/panospao7/Cost-agregator/8b45879e445e8750fcb9210bcf44b9a26e6468dd/scripts/db_guard/scanner.py))
16:
17: The v2 source-evidence verifier has the same limitation: for `direct`, it requires local syntax before each mutation but explicitly makes no dominance or reachability claim. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/panospao7/Cost-agregator/8b45879e445e8750fcb9210bcf44b9a26e6468dd/scripts/db_guard/policy_v2_evidence.py))
18:
19: The acceptance contract requires the stronger property: the barrier must dominate the mutation on the same control-flow path; merely existing elsewhere in the file is a hard failure. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/panospao7/Cost-agregator/8b45879e445e8750fcb9210bcf44b9a26e6468dd/FINAL_CI_GUARD_ACCEPTANCE_GATE.md))
20:
21: ---
22:
23: ## Required predecessors
24:
25: Do not begin unless all are true:
26:
27: ```text
28: GATE-00R completed for the exact start SHA
29: GR-10A canonical command ownership merged
30: GR-10B shared source-scope authority merged
31: GR-10D documentation/evidence truth sync merged
32: GR-11 structural model merged and reproducible
33: Active DB report is trusted
34: Blocking DB diagnostics = 0
35: Advisory DB diagnostics = 0, unless an explicit approved blocker exists
36: No unresolved GR-08 finding batch is changing DB policy concurrently
37: ```
38:
39: Record:
40:
41: ```text
42: START_SHA
43: START_TREE_SHA
44: active policy SHA
45: DB baseline SHA
46: source-root manifest SHA
47: GR-11 structural-shadow report SHA
48: normal DB report SHA
49: ```
50:
51: ## Hard stop conditions
52:
53: Stop; do not weaken policy or downgrade severity if:
54:
55: - GR-11 cannot model a direct-mode callable deterministically;
56: - a canonical barrier receiver cannot be resolved exactly;
57: - the proposed proof relies on a simple class name, token search, filename, wildcard, or sibling overload;
58: - a `runWrite`-like lambda cannot be proven to be the canonical synchronous guard scope;
59: - a direct writer has a real counterexample path;
60: - policy source evidence and D4 would use different proof engines;
61: - direct CLI, ratchet, suite, and Gradle use different policy/root inputs;
62: - another agent owns Gradle.
63:
64: A known invalid direct path is a source-design problem, not a reason to change `direct` into `helper`.
65:

72: - GR-11 structural-analysis modules;
73: - a shared direct-barrier proof module;
74: - exact barrier-receiver/type resolution;
75: - DB scanner and v2 source-evidence integration;
76: - report/protocol/rule-catalog updates;
77: - synthetic Kotlin fixtures and Python tests;
78: - direct-barrier proof inventory and evidence documents;
79: - narrowly related documentation.
80:

82:
83: ```text
84: app/src/main changes
85: DB baseline changes
86: --update-baseline
87: new DB debt baseline entries
88: ownership-policy expansion
89: structural-exception changes
90: helper/worker proof activation
91: call-graph construction beyond direct local proof
92: broad allowlists or exemptions
93: fallback to lexical “barrier-before-text” authorization
94: ```
95:
96: Expected production Kotlin change:
97:
98: ```text
99: no
100: ```

103:
104: # Exact proof contract
105:
106: ## Terms
107:
108: | Term | Meaning |
109: |---|---|
110: | Mutation site | Exact D4-resolved DAO mutation keyed by full policy mutation identity. |
111: | Canonical barrier check | Exact resolved invocation of the approved barrier API that, on normal return, establishes write permission. |
112: | Canonical guarded scope | Exact resolved invocation of approved barrier API whose synchronous lambda body runs only after the barrier check. |
113: | Dominates | Every modeled executable path from callable `ENTRY` to mutation node passes through the barrier node/scope entry. |
114: | Counterexample path | A concrete modeled path from entry to mutation that lacks required barrier evidence. |
115: | Unsupported | The analyzer cannot model the syntax/control flow safely; this is exit `2`, never a clean result. |
116:
117: ## Direct proof outcomes
118:
119: | Outcome | Meaning | Normal DB result |
120: |---|---|---|
121: | `PROVEN` | Exact barrier dominates mutation, or mutation is in canonical guarded scope. | No barrier finding |
122: | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` | CFG is trusted and contains an unguarded path. | Finding / exit `1` |
123: | `UNSUPPORTED` | Required source/control-flow/canonical-call semantics cannot be proven. | Diagnostic / exit `2` |
124: | `INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE` | Policy, roots, source, report, or internal graph invariant failed. | Diagnostic / exit `2` |
125:
126: Never convert `UNSUPPORTED` into `COUNTEREXAMPLE` merely to avoid exit `2`, and never convert `COUNTEREXAMPLE` into advisory.
127:
128: ---
129:
130: # Canonical barrier API contract
131:
132: ## Mandatory discovery before implementation
133:
134: Inspect the real current production barrier API and record its exact facts in:
135:
136: ```text
137: docs/ci/db-structural/GR-12_CANONICAL_BARRIER_API.md
138: ```
139:
140: The record must include:
141:
142: ```text
143: barrier receiver FQCN
144: implementation FQCN(s), if interface-backed
145: exact check method name/signature
146: exact guarded-scope method name/signature
147: lambda parameter/return contract
148: whether invocation is synchronous
149: whether lambda execution is exactly once or at least once
150: normal-return semantics
151: Kotlin test location proving runtime behavior
152: source-evidence location proving implementation identity
153: ```
154:
155: Do not guess an FQCN from the variable name `writeBarrier`.
156:
157: ## Required typed contract
158:
159: Create one code-owned, closed contract, conceptually:
160:
161: ```text
162: CanonicalBarrierContract
163:   receiverFqcn
164:   directCheckCallable
165:   guardedScopeCallable
166:   contractVersion
167: ```
168:
169: Requirements:
170:
171: 1. Receiver type must resolve exactly from lexical/source type evidence.
172: 2. Bare `writeBarrier` spelling is never sufficient.
173: 3. Same-name methods on another receiver are not barriers.
174: 4. An overload not explicitly represented by the contract is not a barrier.
175: 5. Contract source verification must prove the guarded scope checks write permission before invoking its lambda.
176: 6. Contract tests must prove normal return only occurs after the check passes.
177: 7. Contract changes require a dedicated reviewed diff; no YAML wildcard may redefine it.
178:
179: Use a typed code contract rather than a permissive user-editable allowlist.
180:
181: ---
182:
183: # Required shared API
184:
185: GR-11 must already expose immutable callable CFG and mutation observations. Add one pure shared proof API; both source evidence and D4 consume it.
186:
187: Conceptually:
188:
189: ```text
190: prove_direct_barrier(
191:   callableStructuralInput,
192:   cfg,
193:   mutationSites,
194:   canonicalBarrierContract,
195:   typeResolution,
196: ) -> DirectBarrierProofResult
197: ```
198:
199: Minimum result fields:
200:
201: ```text
202: callableKey
203: mutationKey
204: status
205: proofVersion
206: barrierSite
207: mutationSite
208: counterexampleNodeKinds
209: counterexampleLineSequence
210: diagnostics
211: ```
212:
213: Safety requirements:
214:
215: - repository-relative path only;
216: - no raw Kotlin source;
217: - no SQL;
218: - no absolute paths;
219: - no source excerpts longer than bounded identifiers/line numbers;
220: - deterministic ordering by callable key then mutation identity.
221:
222: ---
223:
224: # Proof semantics
225:
226: ## A. Direct check
227:
228: For a canonical direct check:
229:
230: ```text
231: writeBarrier.checkWritesAllowed(...)
232: ```
233:
234: the proof engine may mark the normal successor as “barrier established.”
235:
236: It must not mark these paths guarded:
237:
238: - exceptional edge from the barrier call;
239: - branch that bypasses the call;
240: - lambda/local function that contains the call but is not proven invoked;
241: - sibling scope;
242: - code before the call;
243: - a later loop iteration if the current path can reach mutation before the check.
244:
245: ## B. Canonical guarded scope
246:
247: For a canonical guarded scope:
248:
249: ```text
250: writeBarrier.runWrite { ... mutation ... }
251: ```
252:
253: the mutation is eligible only if all are true:
254:
255: 1. receiver resolves to canonical barrier type;
256: 2. invoked overload exactly matches contract;
257: 3. mutation is inside that exact lambda source span;
258: 4. lambda is not passed onward, stored, returned, or invoked through unknown callback semantics;
259: 5. the contract verifies guard check precedes lambda invocation;
260: 6. no modeled control-flow edge reaches the mutation from outside the guarded lambda scope.
261:
262: ## C. CFG dominance
263:
264: For each direct mutation `m`, calculate domination over GR-11’s modeled CFG.
265:
266: A direct check succeeds only when:
267:
268: ```text
269: barrier node dominates m
270: AND
271: the barrier node lies on the normal-return path
272: AND
273: no unknown/unsupported edge can reach m without the barrier
274: ```
275:
276: A guarded scope succeeds only when the scope entry dominates the mutation and the mutation is inside its exact canonical lambda body.
277:
278: ## D. Required treatment of difficult forms
279:
280: | Form | Required handling |
281: |---|---|
282: | Barrier after mutation | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` |
283: | Barrier only in one branch; mutation reachable after join | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` |
284: | Barrier and mutation both inside same guarded branch | May be `PROVEN` |
285: | Barrier in `else`, mutation in `then` | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` |
286: | Barrier in loop that may execute zero times; mutation after loop | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` |
287: | Barrier before mutation in same loop body | May be `PROVEN` if CFG proves all entries cross it |
288: | Barrier in `catch` only | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` unless every path to mutation reaches it |
289: | Barrier in `finally` after possible mutation | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` |
290: | Unresolved exception flow reaching mutation | `UNSUPPORTED` |
291: | Barrier in unknown callback/lambda | `UNSUPPORTED` |
292: | Barrier in uncalled local function | `COUNTEREXAMPLE` or `UNSUPPORTED`; never proven |
293: | `launch`, `async`, callback, escaping lambda | `UNSUPPORTED` unless future contract explicitly supports it |
294: | Comments/strings containing barrier API names | No marker |
295:
296: ---
297:

376: # Implementation sequence
377:
378: ## Step 1 — Freeze exact pre-state
379:
380: Capture:
381:
382: ```text
383: GATE-00R evidence ID
384: GR-11 shadow report
385: normal DB report
386: active policy hash
387: baseline hash
388: source-root manifest hash
389: ```
390:
391: Run direct DB scan twice. Require equal trusted semantic output.
392:
393: ## Step 2 — Inventory every direct entry
394:
395: Generate the inventory from active policy plus GR-11 mutation observations.
396:
397: Hard failures:
398:
399: - policy direct entry has no exact mutation observation;
400: - observed direct mutation has no policy entry;
401: - one mutation maps to multiple inconsistent direct barrier forms;
402: - direct callable absent from GR-11 corpus;
403: - corpus status is unsupported.
404:
405: Do not start dominance implementation until the inventory is complete.
406:
407: ## Step 3 — Add failing tests before replacing lexical logic
408:
409: Add fixtures covering all required adversarial placements.
410:
411: Required initial red tests:
412:
413: 1. barrier earlier in unrelated `if`;
414: 2. barrier after mutation;
415: 3. barrier in one branch with mutation after join;
416: 4. barrier in unrelated lambda;
417: 5. barrier in uncalled local function;
418: 6. barrier in `finally` after mutation;
419: 7. `runWrite` on wrong receiver;
420: 8. correct API name in comment/string;
421: 9. same method name on fake barrier type;
422: 10. unknown lambda/coroutine escape;
423: 11. exact direct check before mutation;
424: 12. exact canonical scope around mutation.
425:
426: ## Step 4 — Implement canonical receiver resolution
427:
428: Reuse exact declaration/type-resolution infrastructure where possible.
429:
430: Required failure behavior:
431:
432: ```text
433: wrong FQCN → not a barrier
434: ambiguous receiver type → exit 2
435: unresolved receiver type in direct writer → exit 2
436: wrong overload → not a barrier
437: unknown callback/scope contract → exit 2
438: ```
439:
440: No simple-name match such as `WriteBarrier`.
441:
442: ## Step 5 — Implement proof algorithm over GR-11 CFG
443:
444: Implement, test, and validate:
445:
446: 1. reachability from entry;
447: 2. normal and exceptional successor handling;
448: 3. standard dominance calculation;
449: 4. guarded-scope lexical containment;
450: 5. counterexample reconstruction;
451: 6. deterministic proof result ordering;
452: 7. graph/model invariant checking.
453:
454: A counterexample witness must show node kinds and bounded line numbers, for example:
455:
456: ```text
457: ENTRY → BRANCH(false) → MUTATION
458: ```
459:
460: Do not emit raw source.
461:
462: ## Step 6 — Run shadow comparison before activation
463:
464: For every direct-mode mutation, compare:
465:
466: ```text
467: legacy lexical result
468: GR-12 dominance result
469: ```
470:
471: Classify every difference:
472:
473: | Legacy / new | Meaning | Required action |
474: |---|---|---|
475: | pass / proven | expected | retain |
476: | pass / counterexample | real false pass in old guard | source remediation required |
477: | pass / unsupported | old guard made unproved claim | analyzer/design remediation required |
478: | fail / proven | investigate; old scanner false positive | repair only with regression proof |
479: | fail / counterexample | expected violation | source remediation required |
480: | fail / unsupported | analyzer uncertainty | repair analyzer or split work |
481:
482: Do not activate if any direct policy mutation remains `COUNTEREXAMPLE` or `UNSUPPORTED`.
483:
484: ### Important sequencing exception
485:
486: If GR-12 exposes real direct-path counterexamples, create narrowly scoped **GR-14 direct-remediation slices** early, using the frozen proof inventory. Do not merge GR-12 with a knowingly red active gate.
487:
488: ## Step 7 — Replace active lexical enforcement
489:
490: Only after the full direct inventory is `PROVEN`:
491:
492: 1. remove the D4 substring authorization path;
493: 2. remove/replace the source-evidence “line before mutation” check;
494: 3. use the shared proof index;
495: 4. retain stable finding/report behavior;
496: 5. emit a new exact rule for proven unsafe paths.
497:
498: Recommended rule/diagnostic split:
499:
500: ```text
501: DB_MISSING_WRITE_BARRIER
502:   no canonical direct barrier path exists
503:
504: DB_WRITE_BARRIER_NOT_DOMINATING
505:   canonical barrier exists but a modeled unguarded path reaches mutation
506:
507: DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_PROOF_UNSUPPORTED
508:   source structure/call contract cannot be safely modeled
509:
510: DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_RECEIVER_UNRESOLVED
511:   barrier-looking receiver cannot resolve exactly
512:
513: DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_CONTRACT_INVALID
514:   canonical barrier API itself no longer matches verified contract
515: ```
516:
517: The first two are findings/exit `1`; the latter three are blocking diagnostics/exit `2`.
518:
519: ## Step 8 — Prove all control planes consume the same result
520:
521: Run:
522:

543: # Mandatory test matrix
544:
545: ## Dominance correctness
546:
547: 1. sequential check then mutation;
548: 2. mutation then check;
549: 3. check only in true branch; mutation after join;
550: 4. check and mutation both true branch;

553: 7. loop zero iterations;

557: 11. `finally` cases;
558: 12. early return from guarded branch;

560: 14. multiple direct checks.
561:

## 6. CFG capabilities vs. GR-12 needs
- **Exceptional edges: YES (conservative).** Every TRY gets `EdgeKind.EXCEPTION` edges to each CATCH (cfg.py L250), `FINALLY` edges from try+catches to finally (L267–269), `THROW_EXIT` → `EXIT_EXCEPTIONAL` (L292). Exception-type filtering, try-without-catch/finally, unbraced clauses → `DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED` (tokenizer L1049–1202).
- **Loop handling: YES.** `LOOP_HEADER` node with `LOOP_BODY` edge into body, NORMAL back-edge body-last → header (L226–228), `LOOP_EXIT` dangling from header (L230). `continue` → NORMAL edge to enclosing header via `loop_ctx` (L303–306). `break` → LOOP_EXIT to **EXIT_NORMAL** — explicitly a conservative over-approximation "that adds no entry→mutation path" (L295–300). do-while parsed and spliced (tokenizer L905–980, L1334).
- **Dominance: ABSENT.** Stated in cfg.py docstring L7–8 ("Dominance is NOT computed here — that is GR-12's proof step"), model.py L3, parser.py L6, and ADR non-goals L54. No dominator tree, no post-dominator, no path/reachability computation exists anywhere in the package.
- BARRIER_SCOPE outgoing edge is `UNKNOWN` (lambda never assumed synchronous, L272–280); dangling edges connect to the sequence join/exit (L346–347).
## 7. Receiver types / FQCN resolution
- **The structural model resolves no receiver types and does not parse imports.** `BarrierMarker.receiver_fqcn` exists as an optional field but is **always `None`** in GR-11 — explicit policy ("GR-11 resolves no receiver types: None is the honest 'unresolved' receiver", model.py L159–160; barrier_markers.py L6–7, L55, L105). Markers are matched by regex on **masked** text by receiver simple name (`writeBarrier`, `runGuarded*`, or `\w+.\s*(runWrite|checkWritesAllowed)`); there is no import table, no type inference, no PSI. If GR-12 needs exact barrier-receiver FQCNs, that capability does not exist yet.
- **Exact DAO FQCN for mutations does exist** — but only via the D4 scanner, upstream of the structural model: `MutationObservation.dao_fqcn` is resolved by the scanner's existing inventory path (simple-name → unique Room-inventory FQCN matching; `_database_accessor_types` at scanner.py ~L853 maps `@Database` accessor names to a **unique** DAO FQCN, ambiguity fails closed) and flows into `MutationSite.dao_fqcn` / `MutationSite.source_identity` (`daoPath::daoFqcn#operation(params)`).
## Architecture docs/rules
- `docs/architecture/DB_STRUCTURAL_ANALYSIS_MODEL.md` (479 lines) — the governing ADR: closed node/edge/marker/diagnostic vocabularies ("adding a node or edge kind requires extending this ADR", L88–89); shadow-only status header (L3–16: 276 callables, 114 supported, 162 unsupported, 0 infra failures, two runs byte-identical); GR-12 handoff contract (L448–452: GR-12 consumes supported graphs, exact-span mutation sites, barrier markers with paths — and proves/rejects direct barrier dominance); status vocabulary forbids PROVEN_SAFE/DOMINATED (L381–398); report privacy rules (no raw source/SQL/absolute paths).
- Note: ADR L333 says the shadow report schema section is "*Planned — not yet implemented*" — that is stale; `shadow_report.py` + the CLI implement it (including the `graph` payload extension via `include_graphs`).

## Plan match
- matches current code: **yes** (the GR-11 foundation described above exists, is implemented, and matches the ADR; CLI/CLI-tests/corpus/debt all present)
- mismatches: minor only — (a) ADR "Report schema … Planned — not yet implemented" note is stale; (b) declared node kinds `LAMBDA`, `LOCAL_FUNCTION`, `LOOP_BODY`, `UNKNOWN_CONSTRUCT` and edge kind `LAMBDA_DEFERRED` are modeled in the vocabulary but not emitted by the current builder (relevant if GR-12's proof expects to encounter them); (c) `receiver_fqcn` is always `None` — barrier-receiver FQCN resolution is a genuine gap for an exact-receiver dominance rule.
## Risk
- **medium** — the foundation is solid and immutable, but GR-12's dominance proof will operate on a conservative CFG whose break-edge targets EXIT_NORMAL (not the real loop join), whose BARRIER_SCOPE/UNKNOWN edges are deliberately under-specified, and which has no barrier-receiver type resolution; each is a documented GR-11 decision GR-12 must inherit or explicitly extend the ADR for.

## 1. Lexical write-barrier logic in `scripts/db_guard/scanner.py`
### 1a. Entry signature

### 1b. Direct-mode lexical gate — exact lines `3363-3372`
```python
elif any(item.barrier_mode is BarrierMode.DIRECT
for item in matched_entries):
before = masked[start:call.start()]
if not re.search(r"\bwriteBarrier\s*\.\s*(?:checkWritesAllowed|runWrite)\s*\(", before):
findings.append(GuardFinding(
"DB_MISSING_WRITE_BARRIER", "error", declaration.path,
location, call_symbol,
{"dao": observation.dao_fqcn, "operation": observation.operation},
"Database write lacks required barrier evidence",

```
Context `3313-3353`:
* `3318-3333`: `observation = build_mutation_observation(path=declaration.path, owner_fqcn=..., kind=callable_kind, method=call_symbol.name, receiver=..., parameter_types=..., source=source, call_start=call.start(), call_end=call.end(), dao_accessor=receiver, dao_fqcn=dao, operation=operation, mutation_kind=..., source_identity=...)`
* `3334-3335`: `if mutation_observation_sink is not None: mutation_observation_sink.append(observation)`

### 1c. “Masked text before the call” semantics
* `before = masked[start:call.start()]` where `masked` is `mask_kotlin_source(source)` text for the file, `start`/`end` are the enclosing declaration-range offsets being scanned (same `masked` used for all call discovery), `call.start()` is the resolved DAO-mutation call offset.
* Decides “guarded” iff regex `\bwriteBarrier\s*\.\s*(?:checkWritesAllowed|runWrite)\s*\(` matches **anywhere** in `before` (i.e. strictly before the call, within same declaration range from its start).
* No dominance/reachability, no per-mutation line mapping, no branch analysis. No receiver-qualification check here (contrast `policy_parsing._write_barrier_receiver_is_unqualified`); `\b` only. No comment/string forgery possible because `masked` blanks them.
* Fails as `GuardFinding(code="DB_MISSING_WRITE_BARRIER", severity="error")`.
### 1d. Canonical barrier helpers (used by evidence, NOT scanner)
`scripts/db_guard/policy_parsing.py`:
* `1028-1030`: `WRITE_BARRIER_PATTERN = re.compile(r'writeBarrier\s*\.\s*(?:checkWritesAllowed|runWrite)\s*\(')` with comment `1015-1027` stating ONLY `checkWritesAllowed`/`runWrite` count; `writesAllowed()` never counts.
* `1033`: `def _write_barrier_receiver_is_unqualified(masked_line, match_start):` — `1034-1064` rejects `somewriteBarrier`, `foo.writeBarrier`, `foo . writeBarrier`, `foo. /*c*/ writeBarrier` by inspecting masked context before token.
* `1067`: `def _barrier_before_line(lines, fun_start, mutation_lineno):` — `1068-1094` returns True iff REAL unqualified barrier call on statefully-masked lines in `[fun_start, mutation_lineno-1)`. “Only lines strictly before the mutation line are inspected.”
---
## 2. `direct` barrierMode handling in `scripts/db_guard/policy_v2_evidence.py`
### 2a. Key functions
* `1061`: `def _callable_direct_barrier_evidence(text, decl, relative_mutation_linenos):` doc `1062-1082`: “Reuses `policy_parsing._barrier_before_line` — the exact machinery behind the legacy scanner’s MISSING_WRITE_BARRIER gate… For EVERY distinct extracted mutation line, the callable must show a real unqualified `writeBarrier.checkWritesAllowed(...)` / `writeBarrier.runWrite(...)` call strictly between the fun declaration line and that mutation line (statefully comment/string-masked, receiver-aware). Returns `True` only when every mutation is so preceded; any disproof — or an empty position set, or a bodyless declaration — returns `False`.”
* Implementation `1083-1097`: `fun_start = text.count("\n",0,decl.start_offset)`; `body_base_line = text.count("\n",0,decl.end_offset-len(body))`; loop `if not _barrier_before_line(lines, fun_start, body_base_line+relative_lineno+1): return False`.
* `1100`: `def _barrier_metadata_consistent(group, ck, text, decl, mutation_linenos):` doc `1101-1110`: “a `direct` claim requires exact local direct-barrier syntax before EVERY mutation; `helper`/`workerMediated` carry no local requirement… If ANY group member claims `direct`, full per-mutation proof is required (fail closed on mixed-mode groups). No dominance/reachability claims.”
* `1111-1113`: `if not any(m.barrier_mode is BarrierMode.DIRECT for m in group): return True` else `return _callable_direct_barrier_evidence(...)`.
* Call site `1332-1341` inside `_check_mutations` (Stage 3, “evaluated only for an otherwise-clean group”):
```python
if not errors and not _barrier_metadata_consistent(group, ck, text, decl, mutation_linenos):
errors.append(PolicyError(DB_V2_POLICY_BARRIER_METADATA_INCONSISTENT, {"method": ck.method}))
```
### 2b. What it requires / reports
* Requires: otherwise-clean group (required-pair, ambiguity, daoFqcn, unlisted all passed) + every distinct `_extract_mutation_matches` `lineno` in verified body preceded by qualified-syntax barrier. Empty body/empty positions → `False` → fail.
* Pass: no diagnostic; `CallableGroupResult.trusted=True` (zero diagnostics for group), `EvidenceResult.trusted=True` iff zero diagnostics batch-wide (`795-801`).
* Fail: one `EvidenceDiagnostic(code="DB_V2_POLICY_BARRIER_METADATA_INCONSISTENT", context={method})` per failing group; group `trusted=False`, batch `trusted=False`. Code imported `148`, exported `191`, vocabulary-checked in `EvidenceDiagnostic.__post_init__` `216-225`.
* Unsupported: `758-764` non-`FUNCTION` kind → `DB_V2_POLICY_KIND_UNSUPPORTED`; `991-998` bodyless/non-RESOLVED → `DB_V2_POLICY_BODY_UNSUPPORTED`; strict Stage-2 codes `METHOD_MISSING`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`PARSER_UNCERTAIN`/`SIGNATURE_UNRESOLVED`/`MUTATION_NOT_FOUND`/`DAO_AMBIGUOUS`/`DAO_FQCN_MISMATCH`/`UNLISTED_MUTATION` precede barrier gate (emission order comment `1160-1164`).
* Doc contract `588-594` + `34-38`: “`barrierMode` metadata is locally consistency-checked (PR-GR-06 Slice 1)… No dominance/reachability claims are made and mediation is never inferred.”

## 6. DB CLI entrypoints (role / calls / exit codes)
All share universal `0 pass / 1 violation / 2 infra` unless noted.
1. `scripts/verify_db_access_boundaries.py` (header `50-61`, pipeline `14-24`)
* Role: authoritative protocol-v2 DB gate: roots → inventory → active v2 load → v2 evidence → structural manifest → D4 discovery + typed auth → protocol-v2 findings report. Also `--inventory-only`, `--legacy-shadow-report` (report-only, never changes exit).
* Calls: `load_policy_v2` (`631,666-668`), `verify_v2_policy_source_evidence` (`3678`), `build_room_inventory` (`3651`), `scan_db_access` (`3714`) (grep `verify_db_access_boundaries.py: scan_db_access|verify_v2…|load_policy_v2|build_room_inventory`).
* Exits: `0` clean trusted (or inventory-only success); `1` protocol-v2 findings; `2` BLOCKING infra/config diagnostics. Advisory (`controlled_context.advisory`) never breaks trust/exit. `--fail-on-violation` compat only.
2. `scripts/ci/guard_ratchet.py` (header `8-24`)
* Role: no-growth baseline enforcer. Runs guard child (v1 stdout fingerprints or v2 report-file via `COST_AGGREGATOR_GUARD_FINDINGS_FILE`), compares to baseline, optionally `--propose-baseline` candidate.
* Calls: no scanner/evidence directly; executes child (typically `verify_db_access_boundaries.py`) via `run_guard_command`/`run_guard_command_v2` (`638,850`), loads report via `guard_findings.load_report`, aggregates via `aggregate_findings`.
* Exits: `0` no delta (+ proposal written if requested); `1` v1 new/stale (with `--fail-on-violation`) or v2 ANY delta (new/resolved keys/occurrences, independent of flag); `2` infra (crash/missing/malformed baseline, child launch/timeout/unexpected exit, expired debt, blocking report diagnostics; v1 baseline with v2 protocol). Advisory diagnostics never block.
3. `scripts/ci/run_static_guard_suite.py` (header `8-20`)
* Role: runs ALL guards (registry-derived plan via `guard_execution_plan.compile_static_suite_plan`, `SUITE_GUARD_ORDER 196-204`, infra legs `174-181`), writes per-guard logs + `summary.json/md` + `execution-plan.json/.sha256` + `effective-inputs.json` (`462-481`).
* Calls: subprocesses only (no direct scanner/evidence import); db_access leg is ratchet with derived child timeout `max(GUARD_TIMEOUT-60,600)` (`138-147`); guard_tests leg is `pytest scripts/test_verify_*.py scripts/ci/test_*.py`.
* Exits (`762-773` `determine_exit_code`): `0` all blocking passed (slow≠fail); `1` ≥1 blocking violation, no infra; `2` ≥1 infra error.
4. `scripts/ci/gradle_db_guard_inputs.py` (header `1-42`, API `64-78`)
* Role: **library-only** Python contract mirror of retired inline Gradle validation (no CLI `main`, no `sys.exit`). Retained for behavioral tests; live Gradle delegates to plan compiler.
* Calls: none (pure path/version checks). `DEFAULT_DB_GUARD_INPUTS` 7 pairs: `guard_ratchet.py:dbGuardRatchetPath`, `verify_db_access_boundaries.py:dbGuardScriptPath`, `db_access_v2.json:dbGuardBaselinePath`, `db_ownership_policy.yml`, `db_structural_exceptions.yml`, `...expected_methods.yml`, `production_source_roots.yml`.
* Exits/exceptions: raises `GradleDbGuardInputError(code)`: `outside_root`, `not_found`, `not_regular`, `not_readable` (`validate_db_guard_inputs 141-183`), `python_preflight` (`preflight_python_executable 186-217`).
5. `scripts/ci/test_gradle_db_guard_contract.py` — **pytest contract test, not a CLI** (header `1-73`).
* Role: asserts `:app:verifyDbAccessBoundaries` is thin wrapper around `run_registered_guard.py --guard-id db_access --context gradle` (no `--command-arg/--baseline` construction, `pythonInterpreter()` + preflight, 4 typed `--input-override`s gated by `-PdbGuardTestOverrides=true`), plus ratchet/input-mirror behavioral tests on temp fixtures.
* Calls: parses `app/build.gradle.kts`, imports `gradle_db_guard_inputs`, spawns `guard_ratchet.py` fixtures. Exit via pytest (0 pass / non-zero fail), not `0/1/2` guard semantics.
6. `scripts/ci/guard_registry.py` (header `1-44`, `GUARD_REGISTRY 61-816`, `validate_registry 821-880`, `__main__ 885-894`)
* Role: single source of truth: `script/tests/mode/baseline/allowlist/policies/description` + `execution{engine,entrypoint,arguments,mode,requiredInputs,timeoutProfile,outputContract,ratchet,testManifest,documentationAnchor}` + `sourceScope`. `db_access` entry `596-653`: `mode ratchet`, `finding_protocol 2`, `requiredInputs` 4 policy/manifest files, `timeoutProfile D4`, child template with `--fail-on-violation --structural-manifest --ownership-policy --structural-exceptions`.
* Calls: none (data + `validate_registry`). CLI: `python guard_registry.py [root]` → `0` “N guards registered and valid”, `1` on any `REGISTRY ERROR` (note: uses `1`, not `2`).
Related bridge (not asked but wiring): `scripts/ci/run_registered_guard.py:27-30` universal `0/1/2` passthrough; `app/build.gradle.kts:104-131` `runRegisteredGuardFromGradle` maps child `0→pass,1→onViolation,2→onInfra,else→GradleException`.

## 7. Gradle `:app:verifyDbAccessBoundaries`
Definition `app/build.gradle.kts:683-744` (+ shared helpers `12-27,50-75,80-99,104-131`, wiring `746-749`):
```kotlin
tasks.register("verifyDbAccessBoundaries") {
group = "verification"
description = "Fails build if unauthorized direct DAO mutations are found ... (canonical registry plan via run_registered_guard.py; ratchet-enforced, fail closed)"
doLast {
val taskName = "verifyDbAccessBoundaries"
val runnerFile = file("$rootDir/scripts/ci/run_registered_guard.py")
validateRegisteredRunnerInput(taskName, runnerFile)   // 50-75: in-repo + exists/isFile/canRead else GradleException
val pythonExecutable = pythonInterpreter()            // 12-27: -PpythonExecutable → python3 → python → "python3"
pythonPreflightOrThrow(taskName, pythonExecutable)    // 80-99: `executable --version` non-zero/launch-fail → GradleException (infra)
// 693-698: 4 test-only override inputs (policy, structural, manifest, source-roots); each requires -PdbGuardTestOverrides=true else GradleException (703-709); relative resolved vs rootDir (710-714) → --input-override KEY=ABS
// 717: ciArgs = ["--ci-mode"] iff no overrides (runner rejects combo)
runRegisteredGuardFromGradle(taskName, guardId="db_access", pythonExecutable, runnerFile, extraArgs=ciArgs+overrideArgs, onViolation={...}, onInfra={...}) // 104-131: [python, runner, --guard-id db_access, --context gradle, --root <rootDir>] + extras, shell=False; 0→pass,1→violation msg,2→infra msg,else→GradleException
}
}
tasks.named("check") { dependsOn("verifyDbAccessBoundaries"); dependsOn("verifyNoIgnoredGrowth") } // 746-749
```
* How it invokes Python: **never constructs guard/ratchet argv**; delegates to `run_registered_guard.py` which compiles `guard_registry.db_access.execution` via `guard_execution_plan` (validates required inputs/baseline/entrypoint fail-closed pre-launch) then runs ratchet → `verify_db_access_boundaries.py`.
* Input hashes pinned: **none inline in `.kts`**. Since PR-GR-10A Slice 3 “Gradle owns ONLY runner file + interpreter + root + test overrides” (`39-49,666-675`). Required inputs (`db_access.requiredInputs` 4 files + `guard_ratchet.py` + `verify_db_access_boundaries.py` + `db_access_v2.json` per `test_gradle_db_guard_contract.REQUIRED_INPUTS 110-118`) are compiler-validated; hashes land in capture `input-manifest.json`/`input-sha256.txt` and suite `effective-inputs.json`/`execution-plan.sha256`, not in KTS. `gradle_db_guard_inputs.DEFAULT_DB_GUARD_INPUTS` is historical mirror only.
* Outputs: `GradleException` with violation text (“Add exact entry… or structural exception…”) on child 1; infra text (“missing baseline, malformed config, registry/plan-compiler failure, or ratchet failure…”) on child 2; unexpected code → `GradleException`. No files written by task itself.

## 9. Proof bridge? + identity correlation
### 9a. Does any code pass `barrier_markers`/structural results INTO scanner/evidence?

* Structural flow is one-way shadow: `scripts/ci/inspect_db_structural_model.py:42-53` imports `collect_barrier_markers`, `model`, `shadow_report.analyze_callable_structurally/build_shadow_report`; `172` calls `scan_db_access(..., mutation_observation_sink=observations)` to **read** resolved mutations; `301` passes `barrier_marker_fn=collect_barrier_markers` into `analyze_callable_structurally`, which `shadow_report.py:70-78` forwards to `build_callable_cfg(parse, mutation_sites, markers, ...)`. Report is `reportOnly: True` (`shadow_report.py:171-180`, `barrier_markers.py:1-10` “Markers are SYNTAX OBSERVATION, never proof”).
* `scanner.py:2687-2693,3334-3335` sink is append-only observation (same values as `match_mutation`), never consumed for auth/barrier. `cfg.py:1-8` explicitly: “Dominance is NOT computed here — that is GR-12’s proof step.” `model.py:1-5` + `diagnostics.py:1-3` + `parser.py:6` + `tokenizer.py:529, test_tokenizer.py:6` all state “GR-12 owns dominance; GR-11 decides no pass/fail.”
* GR-12 would need to add: dominance proof module + thread `barrier_markers`/CFG verdict into `scan_db_access` lexical gate (`3363-3372`) and `policy_v2_evidence._callable_direct_barrier_evidence/_barrier_metadata_consistent` (`1061-1113,1332-1341`), replacing `re.search(before)` / `_barrier_before_line` per-mutation-line checks.
### 9b. How `policy_v2_evidence` correlates a policy row to a mutation observation
It does **not** consume `MutationObservation`; it re-derives mutations from source and exact-matches identity in two directions (`_check_mutations` `1116-1342`):
* Per group (`entry.callable_key().canonical_key()` grouping `632-634`, sorted `694`), resolve file/owner/kind/callable exactly (`701-1003`), build `class_map + param-bindings + method_map → merged` (`1180-1202`), `matches = _extract_mutation_matches(decl.body, var_map=merged)` (`1203`), `pairs/accessor` via `_resolve_dao_identity` + `_accessor_identity` alias bridge (`1214-1259`).
* Required-pair (policy→source) `1261-1276`: every member’s own `(dao_accessor, operation)` (alias-resolved) must be found among body pairs else `DB_V2_POLICY_MUTATION_NOT_FOUND`.
* Ambiguity `1278-1280`, inventory daoFqcn cross-check `1296-1311` via `_inventory_dao_fqcn_index` (`344-374`: `accessor = lowerFirst(simpleName)`, `{accessor: frozenset(fqcn)}`; mismatch → `DB_V2_POLICY_DAO_FQCN_MISMATCH`), unlisted (source→policy) `1318-1330` else `DB_V2_POLICY_UNLISTED_MUTATION`, then barrier gate `1335-1341`.
* Scanner-side exact identity is `scripts/db_guard/policy_model.py:163-189`:
```python
def match_mutation(entry, *, path, owner_fqcn, kind, receiver, parameter_types, dao_accessor, dao_fqcn, operation, method) -> bool:
"""Exact-match every identity field; no wildcards."""
callable_key = entry.callable_key(); mutation_key = entry.mutation_key()
return (callable_key.path == path and callable_key.owner_fqcn == owner_fqcn and callable_key.kind == CallableKind(kind) and callable_key.method == method and callable_key.receiver == receiver and callable_key.parameter_types == tuple(parameter_types) and mutation_key.dao_accessor == dao_accessor and mutation_key.dao_fqcn == dao_fqcn and mutation_key.operation == operation)
```
`MutationObservation` (`mutation_observation.py:59-84,139-183`) carries the same 9 fields + `callable_key` in exact `canonical_key()` spelling (`path|owner|kind|method|receiver|null|params`) for GR-12 correlation, built at scanner `3318-3333` from identical values passed to `match_mutation`.

6: @Singleton
7: class DatabaseWriteBarrier @Inject constructor(
8:     private val restoreMaintenanceMode: RestoreMaintenanceMode
9: ) {
10:     /** Compatibility overload — kept for existing callers. */
11:     fun checkWritesAllowed(operation: String) {
12:         checkWritesAllowed(DatabaseAccessOperation(operation))
13:     }
14:
15:     fun checkWritesAllowed(operation: DatabaseAccessOperation) {
16:         val mode = restoreMaintenanceMode.currentMode()
17:         if (mode != RestoreMaintenanceMode.Mode.NORMAL) {
18:             throw DatabaseAccessBlockedException(
19:                 accessType = DatabaseAccessType.WRITE,
20:                 operation = operation,
21:                 mode = mode
22:             )
23:         }
24:     }
25:
26:     /**
27:      * Returns true if writes are currently allowed (NORMAL mode).
28:      */
29:     fun writesAllowed(): Boolean {
30:         return restoreMaintenanceMode.isWritesAllowed()
31:     }
32:
33:     suspend fun <T> runWrite(
34:         operation: DatabaseAccessOperation,
35:         block: suspend () -> T
36:     ): T {
37:         checkWritesAllowed(operation)
38:         return block()
39:     }

1: """Authoritative DB ownership policy v2 identity model (PR-01).
2:
3: One entry authorizes exactly one DAO mutation pair in exactly one callable.
4: barrierMode is metadata only in PR-01.
5: """
6:
7: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
8: from enum import Enum
9: from typing import Iterable, Optional, Tuple
10:
11:
12: class CallableKind(str, Enum):
13:     """Identity of the callable that owns a DAO mutation."""
14:
15:     FUNCTION = 'function'
16:     CONSTRUCTOR = 'constructor'
17:     PROPERTY_GETTER = 'property_getter'
18:     PROPERTY_SETTER = 'property_setter'
19:     TOP_LEVEL_FUNCTION = 'top_level_function'
20:     INITIALIZER = 'initializer'
21:
22:
23: class BarrierMode(str, Enum):
24:     """How the owning callable reaches the DAO (metadata only in PR-01)."""
25:
26:     DIRECT = 'direct'
27:     HELPER = 'helper'
28:     WORKER_MEDIATED = 'workerMediated'
29:
30:
31: def _require_non_empty_str(value, label):
32:     if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
33:         raise TypeError('%s must be a non-empty string' % (label,))
34:     return value
35:
36:
37: @dataclass(frozen=True)
38: class CallableKey:
39:     """Immutable identity of exactly one callable."""
40:
41:     path: str
42:     owner_fqcn: str
43:     kind: CallableKind
44:     method: str
45:     receiver: Optional[str]
46:     parameter_types: Tuple[str, ...]
47:
48:     def __post_init__(self):
49:         _require_non_empty_str(self.path, 'path')
50:         _require_non_empty_str(self.owner_fqcn, 'owner_fqcn')
51:         if '.' not in self.owner_fqcn:
52:             raise TypeError('owner_fqcn must contain "."')
53:         if not isinstance(self.kind, CallableKind):
54:             raise TypeError('kind must be a CallableKind')
55:         _require_non_empty_str(self.method, 'method')
56:         if self.receiver is not None and not isinstance(self.receiver, str):
57:             raise TypeError('receiver must be None or a string')
58:         if not isinstance(self.parameter_types, tuple) or not all(
59:             isinstance(item, str) for item in self.parameter_types
60:         ):
61:             raise TypeError('parameter_types must be a tuple of strings')
62:
63:     def canonical_key(self) -> str:
64:         receiver_part = self.receiver if self.receiver is not None else 'null'
65:         return '|'.join(
66:             [
67:                 self.path,
68:                 self.owner_fqcn,
69:                 self.kind.value,
70:                 self.method,
71:                 receiver_part,
72:                 ','.join(self.parameter_types),
73:             ]
74:         )
75:
76:
77: @dataclass(frozen=True)
78: class MutationKey:
79:     """Immutable identity of one DAO mutation inside one callable."""
80:
81:     callable_key: CallableKey
82:     dao_accessor: str
83:     dao_fqcn: str
84:     operation: str
85:
86:     def __post_init__(self):
87:         _require_non_empty_str(self.dao_accessor, 'dao_accessor')
88:         _require_non_empty_str(self.dao_fqcn, 'dao_fqcn')
89:         _require_non_empty_str(self.operation, 'operation')
90:
91:     def canonical_key(self) -> str:
92:         return (
93:             self.callable_key.canonical_key()
94:             + '|'
95:             + self.dao_accessor
96:             + '|'
97:             + self.dao_fqcn
98:             + '|'
99:             + self.operation
100:         )
101:
102:
103: @dataclass(frozen=True)
104: class PolicyEntry:
105:     """One authorized DAO mutation pair in exactly one callable."""
106:
107:     path: str
108:     owner_fqcn: str
109:     kind: CallableKind
110:     method: str
111:     receiver: Optional[str]
112:     parameter_types: Tuple[str, ...]
113:     dao_accessor: str
114:     dao_fqcn: str
115:     operation: str
116:     barrier_mode: BarrierMode
117:     reason: str
118:     owner: str
119:     linked_issue: str
120:
121:     def __post_init__(self):
122:         _require_non_empty_str(self.path, 'path')
123:         _require_non_empty_str(self.owner_fqcn, 'owner_fqcn')
124:         if '.' not in self.owner_fqcn:
125:             raise TypeError('owner_fqcn must contain "."')
126:         if not isinstance(self.kind, CallableKind):
127:             raise TypeError('kind must be a CallableKind')
128:         _require_non_empty_str(self.method, 'method')
129:         if self.receiver is not None and not isinstance(self.receiver, str):
130:             raise TypeError('receiver must be None or a string')
131:         if not isinstance(self.parameter_types, tuple) or not all(
132:             isinstance(item, str) for item in self.parameter_types
133:         ):
134:             raise TypeError('parameter_types must be a tuple of strings')
135:         _require_non_empty_str(self.dao_accessor, 'dao_accessor')
136:         _require_non_empty_str(self.dao_fqcn, 'dao_fqcn')
137:         _require_non_empty_str(self.operation, 'operation')
138:         if not isinstance(self.barrier_mode, BarrierMode):
139:             raise TypeError('barrier_mode must be a BarrierMode')
140:         _require_non_empty_str(self.reason, 'reason')
141:         _require_non_empty_str(self.owner, 'owner')
142:         _require_non_empty_str(self.linked_issue, 'linked_issue')
143:
144:     def callable_key(self) -> CallableKey:
145:         return CallableKey(
146:             path=self.path,
147:             owner_fqcn=self.owner_fqcn,
148:             kind=self.kind,
149:             method=self.method,
150:             receiver=self.receiver,
151:             parameter_types=self.parameter_types,
152:         )
153:
154:     def mutation_key(self) -> MutationKey:
155:         return MutationKey(
156:             callable_key=self.callable_key(),
157:             dao_accessor=self.dao_accessor,
158:             dao_fqcn=self.dao_fqcn,
159:             operation=self.operation,
160:         )
161:
162:
163: def match_mutation(
164:     entry,
165:     *,
166:     path,
167:     owner_fqcn,
168:     kind,
169:     receiver,
170:     parameter_types,
171:     dao_accessor,
172:     dao_fqcn,
173:     operation,
174:     method,
175: ) -> bool:
176:     """Exact-match every identity field; no wildcards."""
177:     callable_key = entry.callable_key()
178:     mutation_key = entry.mutation_key()
179:     return (
180:         callable_key.path == path
181:         and callable_key.owner_fqcn == owner_fqcn
182:         and callable_key.kind == CallableKind(kind)
183:         and callable_key.method == method
184:         and callable_key.receiver == receiver
185:         and callable_key.parameter_types == tuple(parameter_types)
186:         and mutation_key.dao_accessor == dao_accessor
187:         and mutation_key.dao_fqcn == dao_fqcn
188:         and mutation_key.operation == operation
189:     )

Now I'll write the inventory generator. It should:
1. Load policy v2 via load_policy_v2 (from policy_v2_loader).
2. Run scan_db_access with mutation_observation_sink over source root.
3. For each policy entry with barrier_mode == DIRECT, find matching observation(s) via match_mutation. Hard fail if 0 or >1 (per plan: policy direct entry has no exact mutation observation → hard failure; one mutation mapping to multiple inconsistent direct barrier forms → hard failure).
4. Check GR-11 corpus for the callable: load docs/ci/db-structural/GR-11_CORPUS.yml, entry by callableKey; hard fail if absent or UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY.
5. Emit YAML inventory with schema fields per plan.

1: """GR-11 structural shadow CLI (read-only, report-only, opt-in).
2:
3: Runs the conservative structural analysis over every callable that the D4
4: scanner fully resolved as a DAO mutation in the current tree, plus corpus
5: accounting against the active v2 policy.  It NEVER modifies policy,
6: baseline, source, or any active gate result, and it is NOT a ratchet child.
7:
8: Exit contract:
9:   0  every analyzed callable is SUPPORTED and fully accounted for
10:   1  valid analysis with one or more UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY callables
11:   2  infrastructure/correlation failure: invalid policy/roots, an observed
12:      mutation whose callable cannot be correlated to one exact declaration,
13:      or any INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE result
14:
15: The report is deterministic JSON: sorted callables, bounded path/line
16: diagnostics, no raw source, no absolute paths, no timestamps.
17: """

30:     sys.path.insert(0, _PROJECT_ROOT)

57:
58: _EXIT_OK = 0
59: _EXIT_UNSUPPORTED = 1
60: _EXIT_INFRASTRUCTURE = 2
61:
62: _HEADER_NAME_RE = re.compile(r"([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*\(")
63:
64:
65: def _span_of(declaration) -> tuple[int, int]:
66:     """Analysis span of a declaration: braced body, or full declaration span
67:     for expression-bodied members (whose body the scanner records as None)."""
68:     if declaration.body_start is not None and declaration.body_end is not None:
69:         return declaration.body_start, declaration.body_end
70:     return declaration.source_start, declaration.source_end
71:
72:
73: def _expression_body_start(masked: str, start: int, end: int) -> int | None:
74:     """Offset of the expression-body ``=`` of a declaration, or None.
75:
76:     The first standalone ``=`` after the header's parameter list; arrows
77:     (``->``), equality/comparison operators, and defaults inside the header
78:     are skipped by construction (the scan starts after the balanced ``(...)``).
79:     """
80:     open_paren = masked.find("(", start, end)
81:     if open_paren < 0:
82:         return None
83:     depth = 0
84:     close_paren = None
85:     for offset in range(open_paren, end):
86:         character = masked[offset]
87:         if character == "(":
88:             depth += 1
89:         elif character == ")":
90:             depth -= 1
91:             if depth == 0:
92:                 close_paren = offset
93:                 break
94:     if close_paren is None:
95:         return None
96:     for offset in range(close_paren + 1, end):
97:         if masked[offset] != "=":
98:             continue
99:         previous = masked[offset - 1] if offset > 0 else ""
100:         following = masked[offset + 1] if offset + 1 < end else ""
101:         if previous in ("-!", "<>=") or following == "=":
102:             continue
103:         return offset
104:     return None
105:
106:
107: def _project_root_of(root: str) -> str:
108:     supplied = os.path.abspath(os.fspath(root))
109:     parts = os.path.basename(supplied)
110:     if parts == "java" and os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(supplied)) == "main":
111:         return os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(supplied))))
112:     return supplied
113:
114:
115: def _policy_path(root: str, value: str | None) -> str:
116:     candidate = value or os.path.join(root, "config", "guards", "db_ownership_policy.yml")
117:     return candidate if os.path.isabs(candidate) else os.path.join(root, candidate)
118:
119:
120: def _split_callable_key(callable_key: str) -> tuple[str, str, str, str] | None:
121:     """Return (path, owner_fqcn, kind, method) from a canonical key.
122:
123:     The canonical key is ``path|ownerFqcn|kind|method|receiver|params``; the
124:     path is repository-relative and contains no pipes, so the first four
125:     pipe-separated segments are exact.
126:     """
127:     parts = callable_key.split("|")
128:     if len(parts) != 6 or not all(parts[:4]):
129:         return None
130:     return parts[0], parts[1], parts[2], parts[3]
131:
132:
133: def build_structural_shadow(
134:     root: str,
135:     policy_path_value: str | None,
136:     target_sha: str | None = None,
137:     include_graphs: bool = False,
138: ) -> tuple[dict, int]:
139:     """Run the shadow analysis and return (report, exit_code).
140:
141:     Raises nothing by contract: infrastructure failures are encoded in the
142:     report and the exit code.
143:     """
144:     project_root = _project_root_of(root)
145:     policy_file = _policy_path(project_root, policy_path_value)
146:
147:     failure_reasons: list[str] = []
148:     results = []
149:     unaccounted: list[str] = []
150:     corpus_policy_callable_count = 0
151:
152:     root_set, root_diagnostics = resolve_source_root_set(project_root)
153:     if root_set is None or root_diagnostics:
154:         failure_reasons.extend(
155:             sorted({code for code, _context in root_diagnostics})
156:         )
157:     policy_entries = None
158:     if not failure_reasons:
159:         policy_entries, policy_errors = load_policy_v2(policy_file)
160:         if policy_entries is None:
161:             failure_reasons.append("DB_POLICY_SOURCE_EVIDENCE_INVALID")
162:
163:     observations: list[MutationObservation] = []
164:     scan_diagnostic_codes: list[str] = []
165:     scan_finding_count = 0
166:     if not failure_reasons:
167:         scan_result = scan_db_access(
168:             root,
169:             policy_entries,
170:             os.path.join(project_root, "config", "guards", "db_structural_exceptions.yml"),
171:             os.path.join(project_root, "config", "guards", "db_raw_query_classification.yml"),
172:             mutation_observation_sink=observations,
173:         )
174:         # Bounded scan facts only; trust/untrusted decisions stay with the
175:         # normal DB CLI, never with this shadow tool.
176:         scan_diagnostic_codes = sorted(
177:             {diagnostic.code for diagnostic in getattr(scan_result, "diagnostics", ())}
178:         )
179:         scan_finding_count = len(getattr(scan_result, "findings", ()))
180:
181:     if not failure_reasons and policy_entries is not None:
182:         corpus_policy_callable_count = len(
183:             {
184:                 canonical_callable_key(
185:                     entry.path,
186:                     entry.owner_fqcn,
187:                     entry.kind,
188:                     entry.method,
189:                     entry.receiver,
190:                     entry.parameter_types,
191:                 )
192:                 for entry in policy_entries
193:             }
194:         )
195:
196:     declaration_index: dict[tuple[str, str, str], list] = {}
197:     file_text: dict[str, str] = {}
198:     if not failure_reasons:
199:         scan = scan_production_declarations(root, root_set=root_set)
200:
201:         def _callable_name(masked: str, declaration) -> str | None:
202:             # The declaration scanner records no callable_name; the name is
203:             # the identifier before the header's first "(" in masked text.
204:             header_end = (
205:                 declaration.body_start
206:                 if declaration.body_start is not None
207:                 else declaration.source_end
208:             )
209:             header = masked[declaration.source_start:header_end]
210:             match = _HEADER_NAME_RE.search(header)
211:             return match.group(1) if match else None
212:
213:         for declaration in scan.helper_ranges:
214:             if declaration.kind != "function" or declaration.source_start is None:
215:                 # Expression-bodied members (body_start None) stay in the
216:                 # index: their full declaration span supports containment
217:                 # and their analysis fails conservatively as expression-body.
218:                 continue
219:             if declaration.path not in file_text:
220:                 with open(
221:                     os.path.join(project_root, declaration.path),
222:                     "r",
223:                     encoding="utf-8",
224:                 ) as handle:
225:                     file_text[declaration.path] = handle.read()
226:             masked = mask_kotlin_source(file_text[declaration.path])
227:             name = _callable_name(masked, declaration)
228:             if name is None:
229:                 continue
230:             # Policy CallableKind spellings (function, top_level_function,
231:             # property_getter, ...) do not map 1:1 onto declaration kinds, so
232:             # correlation keys on identity only and disambiguates by
233:             # parameter tuple; ambiguity fails closed below.
234:             declaration_index.setdefault(
235:                 (declaration.path, declaration.owner_fqcn, name),
236:                 [],
237:             ).append(declaration)
238:
239:         for observation in observations:
240:             identity = _split_callable_key(observation.callable_key)
241:             if identity is None:
242:                 unaccounted.append(observation.callable_key)
243:                 continue
244:             path, owner_fqcn, _kind, method = identity
245:             candidates = declaration_index.get((path, owner_fqcn, method), [])
246:             # The declaration scanner records no parameter tuples, so
247:             # overloads are disambiguated by EXACT span containment: the
248:             # observed DAO call offset must fall inside exactly one same-name
249:             # declaration's body.  Anything else fails closed.
250:             containing = [
251:                 item
252:                 for item in candidates
253:                 if _span_of(item)[0] <= observation.source_start < _span_of(item)[1]
254:             ]
255:             if len(containing) != 1:
256:                 # Correlation is tooling, not source syntax: an observed
257:                 # mutation that cannot be matched to exactly one declared
258:                 # callable is an infrastructure failure, never "unsupported".
259:                 unaccounted.append(observation.callable_key)
260:                 continue
261:             declaration = containing[0]
262:             if declaration.path not in file_text:
263:                 with open(
264:                     os.path.join(project_root, declaration.path), "r", encoding="utf-8"
265:                 ) as handle:
266:                     file_text[declaration.path] = handle.read()
267:             masked = mask_kotlin_source(file_text[declaration.path])
268:             if declaration.body_start is not None:
269:                 body_start, body_end = declaration.body_start, declaration.body_end
270:             else:
271:                 # Expression-bodied member: the declaration scanner records
272:                 # no braced body.  Aim the span at the "=" so the tokenizer
273:                 # fails conservatively with the expression-body reason —
274:                 # never silently skipped.
275:                 expression_start = _expression_body_start(
276:                     masked, declaration.source_start, declaration.source_end
277:                 )
278:                 if expression_start is None:
279:                     unaccounted.append(observation.callable_key)
280:                     continue
281:                 body_start, body_end = expression_start, declaration.source_end
282:             body_line = masked.count("\n", 0, body_start) + 1
283:             body_span = SourceSpan(
284:                 start=body_start,
285:                 end=body_end,
286:                 line=body_line,
287:                 column=1,
288:             )
289:             sites = [
290:                 MutationSite.from_observation(item)
291:                 for item in observations
292:                 if item.callable_key == observation.callable_key
293:                 and item.path == declaration.path
294:             ]
295:             result = analyze_callable_structurally(
296:                 masked,
297:                 body_span,
298:                 path=declaration.path,
299:                 callable_key=observation.callable_key,
300:                 mutation_sites=sites,
301:                 barrier_marker_fn=collect_barrier_markers,
302:             )
303:             results.append(result)
304:
305:     results.sort(key=lambda item: item.callable_key)
306:     seen_keys: set[str] = set()
307:     deduped_results = []
308:     for result in results:
309:         if result.callable_key in seen_keys:
310:             continue
311:         seen_keys.add(result.callable_key)
312:         deduped_results.append(result)
313:
314:     analyzed = {result.callable_key for result in deduped_results}
315:     unaccounted = sorted(set(unaccounted) | (set() if failure_reasons else set()))
316:     report = build_shadow_report(deduped_results, target_sha=target_sha, include_graphs=include_graphs)
317:     report["corpus"] = {
318:         "policyCallableCount": corpus_policy_callable_count,
319:         "observedCallableCount": len(analyzed),
320:         "unaccountedCallableKeys": unaccounted,
321:         "scanFindingCount": scan_finding_count,
322:         "scanDiagnosticCodes": scan_diagnostic_codes,
323:     }
324:     report["infrastructure"] = {
325:         "failureReasons": sorted(set(failure_reasons)),
326:     }
327:
328:     if failure_reasons or unaccounted:
329:         return report, _EXIT_INFRASTRUCTURE
330:     if any(
331:         result.status is AnalysisStatus.INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE
332:         for result in deduped_results
333:     ):
334:         return report, _EXIT_INFRASTRUCTURE
335:     if any(
336:         result.status is AnalysisStatus.UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY
337:         for result in deduped_results
338:     ):
339:         return report, _EXIT_UNSUPPORTED
340:     return report, _EXIT_OK
341:
342:
343: def _write_report_atomic(path: str, report: dict) -> None:
344:     directory = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(path))
345:     os.makedirs(directory, exist_ok=True)
346:     temporary = path + ".tmp"
347:     with open(temporary, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as handle:
348:         json.dump(report, handle, indent=2)
349:         handle.write("\n")

GR00-worktree\scripts\db_guard\structural_analysis\barrier_markers.py
Read 1/1 file, 119 lines
1: """Barrier-marker extraction over a parsed callable body (shadow-only).
2:
3: Markers are SYNTAX OBSERVATION, never proof: regions recognized by the
4: tokenizer carry their own candidate kind (DIRECT_CHECK, DIRECT_SCOPE,
5: WORKER_GUARD_CANDIDATE) and become markers directly; any other barrier-like
6: call shape in the masked text stays UNKNOWN_BARRIER_LIKE_CALL.  Receiver
7: types are not resolved in GR-11, so ``receiver_fqcn`` is always None — an
8: honest unknown.  Markers never come from comments/strings (input is masked)
9: and never duplicate a recognized region's span.
10: """
11: from __future__ import annotations
12:
13: import re
14:
15: from .model import BarrierMarker, BarrierMarkerKind, SourceSpan
16: from .tokenizer import CallableBodyParse, RegionKind
17:
18: __all__ = ["collect_barrier_markers"]
19:
20: _WORKER_GUARD_RE = re.compile(
21:     r"\b[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\s*\.\s*"
22:     r"(?P<method>runGuardedWithContext|runGuarded)\s*(?:\([^()]*\))?\s*[\(\{]"
23: )
24: _BARRIER_LIKE_RE = re.compile(
25:     r"\b(?P<receiver>[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*\.\s*"
26:     r"(?P<method>runWrite|checkWritesAllowed)\s*[\(\{]"
27: )
28:
29: _REGION_METHODS = (
30:     ("runGuardedWithContext", "runGuardedWithContext"),
31:     ("runGuarded", "runGuarded"),
32:     ("runWrite", "runWrite"),
33:     ("checkWritesAllowed", "checkWritesAllowed"),
34: )
35:
36:
37: def _method_in_span(masked_text: str, span: SourceSpan) -> str:
38:     text = masked_text[span.start : span.end]
39:     for needle, method in _REGION_METHODS:
40:         if needle in text:
41:             return method
42:     return "unknown"
43:
44:
45: def _collect_regions(parse: CallableBodyParse, masked_text: str) -> list[BarrierMarker]:
46:     markers: list[BarrierMarker] = []
47:
48:     def walk(regions) -> None:
49:         for region in regions:
50:             if region.barrier is not None:
51:                 markers.append(
52:                     BarrierMarker(
53:                         kind=region.barrier,
54:                         span=region.span,
55:                         receiver_fqcn=None,
56:                         method=_method_in_span(masked_text, region.span),
57:                     )
58:                 )
59:             walk(region.children)
60:
61:     walk(parse.regions)
62:     return markers
63:
64:
65: def collect_barrier_markers(
66:     parse: CallableBodyParse, masked_text: str
67: ) -> tuple[BarrierMarker, ...]:
68:     """Collect every barrier marker for one parsed callable body.
69:
70:     ``masked_text`` must be the same masked text the parse came from.  Text
71:     occurrences inside a recognized barrier region are not re-reported; any
72:     other barrier-like call shape is UNKNOWN_BARRIER_LIKE_CALL (explicit
73:     uncertainty, never a successful marker).  Deterministic: sorted by
74:     (span.start, span.end, kind, method).
75:     """
76:     markers = _collect_regions(parse, masked_text)
77:     covered = [(marker.span.start, marker.span.end) for marker in markers]
78:
79:     def inside_recognized(start: int, end: int) -> bool:
80:         return any(rec_start <= start and end <= rec_end for rec_start, rec_end in covered)
81:
82:     for pattern, kind in (
83:         (_WORKER_GUARD_RE, BarrierMarkerKind.WORKER_GUARD_CANDIDATE),
84:         (_BARRIER_LIKE_RE, BarrierMarkerKind.UNKNOWN_BARRIER_LIKE_CALL),
85:     ):
86:         for match in pattern.finditer(masked_text, parse.body_span.start, parse.body_span.end):
87:             if inside_recognized(match.start(), match.end()):
88:                 continue
89:             if (
90:                 kind is BarrierMarkerKind.UNKNOWN_BARRIER_LIKE_CALL
91:                 and match.group("receiver") == "writeBarrier"
92:             ):
93:                 # A canonical-receiver call inside an unmodelable region is
94:                 # silence, never a mislabeled unknown marker.
95:                 continue
96:             markers.append(
97:                 BarrierMarker(
98:                     kind=kind,
99:                     span=SourceSpan(
100:                         start=match.start(),
101:                         end=match.end(),
102:                         line=_line_at(masked_text, match.start()),
103:                         column=match.start() - _line_start(masked_text, match.start()) + 1,
104:                     ),
105:                     receiver_fqcn=None,
106:                     method=match.group("method"),
107:                 )
108:             )
109:
110:     markers.sort(key=lambda item: (item.span.start, item.span.end, item.kind.value, item.method))
111:     return tuple(markers)
112:
113:
114: def _line_start(text: str, offset: int) -> int:
115:     return text.rfind("\n", 0, offset) + 1
116:
117:
118: def _line_at(text: str, offset: int) -> int:
119:     return text.count("\n", 0, offset) + 1

1049: def _parse_try(
1050:     cur: _Cursor,
1051:     base: int,
1052:     stmt_e: int,
1053:     parts: list[tuple[int, int]],
1054:     idx: int,
1055:     in_lambda: bool,
1056: ) -> tuple[ParsedRegion | None, int]:
1057:     text = cur.text
1058:     stripped = text[base:stmt_e]
1059:     rest = stripped[3:]
1060:     if not rest.lstrip(_WS).startswith("{"):
1061:         cur.fail(
1062:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED",
1063:             base,
1064:             stmt_e,
1065:             "try-without-braces",
1066:         )
1067:         return None, idx + 1
1068:     brace_abs = base + stripped.find("{")
1069:     close = _match_forward(text, brace_abs, stmt_e)
1070:     tail = text[close:stmt_e].strip(_WS) if close > 0 else ""
1071:     if close < 0 or tail:
1072:         cur.fail(
1073:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED"
1074:             if close > 0
1075:             else "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
1076:             base,
1077:             stmt_e,
1078:             "try-body-not-closed" if close > 0 else "unbalanced-brace",
1079:         )
1080:         return None, idx + 1

1082:         ParsedRegion(
1083:             kind=RegionKind.TRY,
1084:             span=cur.span(base, close),
1085:             children=tuple(_parse_sequence(cur, brace_abs + 1, close - 1, in_lambda)),
1086:         )

1089:     nxt = idx + 1
1090:     saw_catch_or_finally = False

1095:         if _leading_kw(nstr, "catch"):

1098:             paren_rel = nstripped.find("(")
1099:             if paren_rel < 0:

1102:                     nbase,

1104:                     "catch-without-param",

1109:             if head_end < 0:
1110:                 cur.fail(

1112:                     paren_abs,
1113:                     ne,

1115:                 )

1121:                     nbase,
1122:                     ne,

1124:                 )

1134:                     nbase,
1135:                     ne,
1136:                     "catch-body-not-closed" if c_close > 0 else "unbalanced-brace",
1137:                 )
1138:                 return None, nxt + 1

1141:                     kind=RegionKind.CATCH,

1145:                     ),
1146:                 )

1149:             nxt += 1

1153:             frest = nstripped[7:]
1154:             if not frest.lstrip(_WS).startswith("{"):
1155:                 cur.fail(
1156:                     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED",
1157:                     nbase,
1158:                     ne,
1159:                     "finally-without-braces",
1160:                 )
1161:                 return None, nxt + 1
1162:             f_brace = nbase + nstripped.find("{")

1170:                     nbase,
1171:                     ne,

1173:                 )
1174:                 return None, nxt + 1
1175:             children.append(
1176:                 ParsedRegion(
1177:                     kind=RegionKind.FINALLY,
1178:                     span=cur.span(nbase, f_close),
1179:                     children=tuple(

1181:                     ),
1182:                 )

1195:         )

Thinking
The try parser handles try/catch/finally with braces, requiring catch-with-param. Rejections occur for:
- try-without-braces
- catch-without-param (Kotlin `catch (e: Exception)` has param; but multi-catch `catch (e: Foo | Bar)`? still has param — fine)
- catch/finally-without-braces
- try-without-catch-finally (Kotlin `try { } finally { }` is fine... try-without-catch-finally means bare try block — actually Kotlin allows `try { }` alone? No — try must have catch or finally. Hmm, bare `try {}` is legal Kotlin? Yes! `try { expr }` without catch/finally is valid Kotlin (used as expression). So rejection is conservative.)
- unbalanced-brace
But the actual rejection reason for the failing callables may not be in the try parser — could be the statement splitter failing to segment try (parts). The corpus reason for BudgetRepository.addBudget is DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED. Thinking
Excellent — precise failure taxonomy for the 8 direct callables:
1. **`dangling-clause` (2 callables: BudgetRepository.addBudget/deleteBudget, ReceiptLifecycleCoordinator.deleteReceipt)** — the statement splitter doesn't attach `catch` to its preceding `try` when the try statement's "part" ends before the catch clause starts. The `try` is probably a multi-line `try { ... } ` where the closing brace is followed by a newline and then `catch (...)` on the next statement boundary. Actually the reason is in a different place than _parse_try — "dangling-clause" appears in the statement segmentation: the catch clause is treated as its own statement (a "clause" that dangles without a try). Need to find where 'dangling-clause' is emitted in tokenizer.py.
2. **`lambda-escape` (PlannedExpenseRepository ×3 + ExchangeRateStoreAdapter.insertOrUpdateAll)** — the parser treats trailing lambda arguments (require(...) { }, map { }, emitPlannedDiagnostic(...) with... wait, emitPlannedDiagnostic isn't a lambda — the span shows a call with named args; maybe the parser mis-detects lambda due to braces in arguments? `require(x) { message }` IS a trailing lambda (require's arg2 is a lambda: `require(value) { lazyMessage }`). Kotlin stdlib `require` takes a lambda. `rates.map { it.toEntity() }` is a lambda. `emitPlannedDiagnostic(...)` — span starts at emitPlannedDiagnostic call... maybe because inside its args there's a `{ }`? Or the parser sees the `(` unbalanced? Whatever — the parser bails conservatively when it can't statically know a lambda is invoked exactly once synchronously (map: invoked once per element; require: invoked only on failure!). These are REAL semantics questions:
- `require(...) { }` — lambda invoked zero or one times. But the mutation isn't inside the lambda; the lambda is an argument. The conservative rejection is because a lambda body may contain arbitrary calls — but the scanner only cares about DAO mutations inside the callable. A lambda argument that doesn't contain the mutation still creates escape uncertainty for the *parser's statement model*, not for the mutation path.
The GR-11 design chose: lambda → escape → unsupported. To model these safely, GR-12 could support "argument lambdas that are syntactically known to be invoked synchronously 0+ times by a KNOWN function" — but that requires a callable contract for require/map etc. That's a slippery slope (plan hard stop: "a runWrite-like lambda cannot be proven to be the canonical synchronous guard scope" — but that's for barrier scope; for a plain lambda argument containing no mutation, the escape concern is whether the mutation inside the lambda runs under unknown semantics).
Key insight: For PROOF purposes, what matters is: (a) mutation sites' paths from entry; (b) barrier dominance. A lambda argument whose body contains NO mutation and NO barrier can be modeled as an opaque STATEMENT (the call happens, lambda content unknown) — that's still conservative for mutations OUTSIDE the lambda. The danger: a mutation INSIDE the lambda body. So the conservative extension: if a lambda's body contains no mutation sites and no barrier markers, model the whole call as a single STATEMENT node (lambda contents opaque = edge UNKNOWN? No — opaque statement is fine because nothing relevant is inside). If a lambda body contains a mutation or barrier marker → remain UNSUPPORTED (lambda-escape). This preserves soundness: we never prove dominance through unmodeled mutation paths because mutations inside lambdas stay unsupported.
Is that consistent with GR-11's philosophy? Yes — conservative, fail-closed: only downgrade to supported when no relevant content is hidden. And the corpus regeneration would move these callables to SUPPORTED if their lambdas are mutation-free.
Wait, but `rates.map { it.toEntity() }` is the ARGUMENT of the mutation call itself: `exchangeRateDao.insertOrUpdateAll(rates.map { it.toEntity() })`. The mutation call contains a lambda argument. The mutation node is the call; the lambda is inside the argument expression. No barrier/mutation inside the lambda → safe to model as a plain statement.
3. **`unbraced-if-body` (WorkerExecutionGuard.recoverStaleRunningJobs)** — `if (affected == 1) recovered++`. Extending the tokenizer to parse unbraced if/else bodies as single-statement regions is straightforward and conservative (the body is one statement — parse it as a sequence of statements with its own region node).
So the capability extension has three parts:
A. Statement segmentation fix: attach `catch`/`finally` clauses to the preceding try when separated (dangling-clause). Need to find where 'dangling-clause' is emitted.
B. Unbraced if/else bodies → parse single-statement body.
C. Lambda arguments: if lambda body contains no mutation/barrier content → treat as opaque; else → keep lambda-escape failure.
Wait — but B and C change the conservative model; the corpus for ALL 276 callables will shift (some previously unsupported become supported). That's the intended capability decision. The ADR says adding node kinds requires extending the ADR — we're not adding kinds, just reducing conservative rejections.
Also, do the mutations in these callables actually sit outside lambdas? PlannedExpenseRepository.addPlannedExpense — the DAO mutations (insert etc.) are presumably in the main body, not inside the require lambdas. Need to verify: for each callable, after extension, mutation sites must be within modeled regions. The proof engine requires mutation sites inside the CFG. If a mutation is inside a lambda → unsupported (GR-14).
Also the 5 unobserved rows: BudgetRepository.addBudget's two insertAndActivate* rows and updateBudget's updateAndEnforceActiveScope, CategoryRepository addCategory/deleteCategory rows. Why 0 observations? Possibly the callable's mutations happen inside a private helper (authorized as separate rows?) — no, these rows' callableKey points to addBudget etc. If the scanner didn't resolve those mutation call sites as observations... DB_SIGNATURE_UNRESOLVED (overload ambiguity) is the likely cause: e.g., insertAndActivateCategory overload ambiguous. Hmm wait — if the signature can't be resolved, the scanner emits the advisory and skips the call (no observation, no finding). Policy evidence module Stage 3 still passes because it resolves differently? Or those rows are stale? The evidence module required-pair check passes for the group (addBudget group includes those rows and they were found in source body text). So the scanner's D4 resolution differs from the evidence module's extraction. This is a scanner capability question (signature resolution for those specific call shapes).
