229: def _split_statements(cur: _Cursor, start: int, end: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]] | None:
230:     parts: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
231:     depth = 0
232:     stmt_start: int | None = None
233:     i = start
234:     text = cur.text
235:     while i < end:
236:         ch = text[i]
237:         if ch not in _WS and stmt_start is None:
238:             stmt_start = i
239:         if ch in "([{":
240:             depth += 1
241:         elif ch in ")]}":
242:             depth -= 1
243:             if depth < 0:
244:                 bad = stmt_start if stmt_start is not None else i
245:                 cur.fail(
246:                     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
247:                     bad,
248:                     i + 1,
249:                     "stray-closing-delimiter",
250:                 )
251:                 return None
252:             if depth == 0 and ch == "}" and stmt_start is not None:
253:                 nxt = cur.non_ws(i + 1, end)
254:                 if nxt < end and _at_clause(text, nxt, end):
255:                     parts.append((stmt_start, i + 1))
256:                     stmt_start = None
257:         if depth == 0 and stmt_start is not None:
258:             if ch == ";":
259:                 parts.append((stmt_start, i))
260:                 stmt_start = None
261:             elif ch == "\n":
262:                 prev = text[stmt_start:i].rstrip(_WS)
263:                 nxt = cur.non_ws(i + 1, end)
264:                 cont = bool(prev) and prev[-1] in _CONT_END
265:                 dot_cont = nxt < end and text[nxt] == "."
266:                 if not cont and not dot_cont:
267:                     parts.append((stmt_start, i))
268:                     stmt_start = None
269:         i += 1
270:     if depth != 0:
271:         bad = stmt_start if stmt_start is not None else start
272:         cur.fail(
273:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED", bad, end, "unbalanced-delimiter"
274:         )
275:         return None
276:     if stmt_start is not None:
277:         tail = text[stmt_start:end]
278:         if tail.strip(_WS):
279:             parts.append((stmt_start, end))
280:     return parts

1: """Shadow-only conservative Kotlin statement-boundary tokenizer.
2:
3: Operates only on masked text (comments/strings blanked, offsets preserved).
4: Recognizes a small supported subset; anything else is an explicit
5: unsupported finding. Spans and kinds only, no raw source retained.
6: """
7: from __future__ import annotations
8:
9: import re
10: from dataclasses import dataclass
11: from enum import Enum
12:
13: from .diagnostics import DIAGNOSTIC_CODES
14: from .model import BarrierMarkerKind, SourceSpan
15:
16: __all__ = [
17:     "RegionKind",
18:     "ParsedRegion",
19:     "UnsupportedFinding",
20:     "CallableBodyParse",
21:     "parse_callable_body",
22: ]
23:
24: _ALLOWED_CODES = (
25:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
26:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
27:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_CONTROL_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED",
28:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_LAMBDA_ESCAPE",
29:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED",
30:     "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BARRIER_FORM_UNRECOGNIZED",
31: )
32:
33: _ID = r"[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*"
34: _RE_FUN = re.compile(r"\bfun\b")
35: _RE_OBJECT = re.compile(r"\bobject\s*(?::|\{)")
36: _RE_COROUTINE = re.compile(
37:     r"(?:^|[^\w$.])(?:launch|async|withContext|runBlocking)\s*[\(\{]"
38: )
39: _RE_BARRIER_SCOPE = re.compile(
40:     r"\bwriteBarrier\s*\.\s*runWrite\s*\{"
41: )
42: _RE_BARRIER_CHECK = re.compile(
43:     r"\bwriteBarrier\s*\.\s*checkWritesAllowed\s*\("
44: )
45: _RE_WORKER_GUARD = re.compile(
46:     r"\b[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\s*\.\s*"
47:     r"(?P<method>runGuardedWithContext|runGuarded)\s*(?:\([^()]*\))?\s*\{"
48: )
49: _RE_LIKE_BARRIER = re.compile(
50:     r"\b\w+\s*\.\s*(?:runWrite|checkWritesAllowed)\s*[\(\{]"
51: )
52: _RE_RETURN_LABEL = re.compile(r"\breturn\s*@")
53: _RE_BREAK_CONT_LABEL = re.compile(r"\b(?:break|continue)\s*@")
54: _RE_IF = re.compile(r"if\s*\(")
55: _RE_WHEN = re.compile(r"when\s*\(")
56: _RE_WHILE = re.compile(r"while\s*\(")
57: _RE_FOR = re.compile(r"for\s*\(")
58: _RE_TRY = re.compile(r"try\s*\{")
59: _RE_CATCH = re.compile(r"catch\s*\(")
60: _RE_FINALLY = re.compile(r"finally\s*\{")
61: _RE_DO = re.compile(r"do\s*\{")
62: _RE_ELSE = re.compile(r"else\b")
63: _RE_ACCESSOR = re.compile(r"(?:^|[^\w])(?:get|set)\s*\(")
64: _RE_JUMP = re.compile(r"^(return|throw|break|continue)(?![\w])")
65:
66: _CONT_END = set("+-*/%,:?.&|<>!(`[")
67: _WS = " \t\r\n\x0b\x0c"
68:
69:
70: class RegionKind(str, Enum):
71:     STATEMENT = "STATEMENT"
72:     BLOCK = "BLOCK"
73:     IF = "IF"
74:     WHEN = "WHEN"
75:     WHEN_BRANCH = "WHEN_BRANCH"
76:     LOOP = "LOOP"
77:     TRY = "TRY"
78:     CATCH = "CATCH"
79:     FINALLY = "FINALLY"
80:     RETURN = "RETURN"
81:     THROW = "THROW"
82:     BREAK = "BREAK"
83:     CONTINUE = "CONTINUE"
84:     ACCESSOR = "ACCESSOR"
85:     BARRIER_SCOPE = "BARRIER_SCOPE"
86:     DIRECT_CHECK = "DIRECT_CHECK"
87:
88:
89: @dataclass(frozen=True)
90: class ParsedRegion:
91:     kind: RegionKind
92:     span: SourceSpan
93:     children: tuple[ParsedRegion, ...] = ()
94:     barrier: BarrierMarkerKind | None = None
95:
96:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
97:         if not isinstance(self.kind, RegionKind):
98:             raise TypeError("ParsedRegion.kind must be a RegionKind")
99:         if not isinstance(self.span, SourceSpan):
100:             raise TypeError("ParsedRegion.span must be a SourceSpan")
101:         if not isinstance(self.children, (tuple, list)):
102:             raise TypeError("ParsedRegion.children must be a tuple of ParsedRegion")
103:         for child in self.children:
104:             if not isinstance(child, ParsedRegion):
105:                 raise TypeError("ParsedRegion.children must be a tuple of ParsedRegion")
106:         if self.barrier is not None and not isinstance(self.barrier, BarrierMarkerKind):
107:             raise TypeError("ParsedRegion.barrier must be a BarrierMarkerKind or None")
108:         object.__setattr__(self, "children", tuple(self.children))
109:
110:
111: @dataclass(frozen=True)
112: class UnsupportedFinding:
113:     code: str
114:     span: SourceSpan
115:     reason: str
116:
117:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
118:         if self.code not in DIAGNOSTIC_CODES or self.code not in _ALLOWED_CODES:
119:             raise ValueError("UnsupportedFinding.code must be an allowed code: %r" % (self.code,))
120:         if not isinstance(self.span, SourceSpan):
121:             raise TypeError("UnsupportedFinding.span must be a SourceSpan")
122:         if not isinstance(self.reason, str) or not self.reason:
123:             raise TypeError("UnsupportedFinding.reason must be a non-empty string")
124:
125:
126: @dataclass(frozen=True)
127: class CallableBodyParse:
128:     body_span: SourceSpan
129:     regions: tuple[ParsedRegion, ...]
130:     unsupported: tuple[UnsupportedFinding, ...]
131:
132:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
133:         if not isinstance(self.body_span, SourceSpan):
134:             raise TypeError("CallableBodyParse.body_span must be a SourceSpan")
135:         if not isinstance(self.regions, (tuple, list)):
136:             raise TypeError("CallableBodyParse.regions must be a tuple of ParsedRegion")
137:         if not isinstance(self.unsupported, (tuple, list)):
138:             raise TypeError("CallableBodyParse.unsupported must be a tuple of UnsupportedFinding")
139:         for region in self.regions:
140:             if not isinstance(region, ParsedRegion):
141:                 raise TypeError("CallableBodyParse.regions must be a tuple of ParsedRegion")
142:         for finding in self.unsupported:
143:             if not isinstance(finding, UnsupportedFinding):
144:                 raise TypeError(
145:                     "CallableBodyParse.unsupported must be a tuple of UnsupportedFinding"
146:                 )
147:         object.__setattr__(
148:             self, "regions", tuple(sorted(self.regions, key=lambda r: (r.span.start, r.span.end)))
149:         )
150:         object.__setattr__(
151:             self,
152:             "unsupported",
153:             tuple(sorted(self.unsupported, key=lambda f: (f.span.start, f.span.end, f.reason))),
154:         )
155:
156:     @property
157:     def is_supported(self) -> bool:
158:         return not self.unsupported
159:
160:
161: def _line_starts(text: str) -> list[int]:
162:     starts = [0]
163:     for match in re.finditer(r"\n", text):
164:         starts.append(match.end())
165:     return starts
166:
167:
168: class _Cursor:
169:     def __init__(self, text: str) -> None:
170:         self.text = text
171:         self.starts = _line_starts(text)
172:         self.findings: list[UnsupportedFinding] = []
173:
174:     def span(self, start: int, end: int) -> SourceSpan:
175:         import bisect
176:
177:         if end < start:
178:             end = start
179:         idx = bisect.bisect_right(self.starts, start) - 1
180:         line = idx + 1
181:         column = start - self.starts[idx] + 1
182:         return SourceSpan(start=start, end=end, line=line, column=column)
183:
184:     def fail(self, code: str, start: int, end: int, reason: str) -> None:
185:         self.findings.append(UnsupportedFinding(code=code, span=self.span(start, end), reason=reason))
186:
187:     def non_ws(self, pos: int, end: int, forward: bool = True) -> int:
188:         if forward:
189:             while pos < end and self.text[pos] in _WS:
190:                 pos += 1
191:             return pos
192:         pos -= 1
193:         while pos >= end and self.text[pos] in _WS:
194:             pos -= 1
195:         return pos + 1
196:
197:
198: def _match_forward(text: str, opening: int, end: int) -> int:
199:     pairs = {"(": ")", "[": "]", "{": "}"}
200:     stack = [text[opening]]
201:     i = opening + 1
202:     while i < end:
203:         ch = text[i]
204:         if ch in pairs:
205:             stack.append(ch)
206:         elif ch in ")]}":
207:             if not stack or pairs[stack[-1]] != ch:
208:                 return -1
209:             stack.pop()
210:             if not stack:
211:                 return i + 1
212:         i += 1
213:     return -1
214:
215:
216: def _at_clause(text: str, pos: int, end: int) -> bool:
217:     """True when ``pos`` starts a clause keyword that continues the enclosing
218:     construct (else/catch/finally/while) or an accessor (get/set): a ``}``
219:     closing to depth 0 right before one of these ends the statement part so
220:     the clause parser sees the clause as its own part."""
221:     for word in ("else", "catch", "finally", "while", "get", "set"):
222:         if text.startswith(word, pos):
223:             after = pos + len(word)
224:             if after >= end or not (text[after].isalnum() or text[after] == "_"):
225:                 return True
226:     return False
227:
228:
229: def _split_statements(cur: _Cursor, start: int, end: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]] | None:
230:     parts: list[tuple[int, int]] = []

658: def _advance_past(
659:     parts: list[tuple[int, int]], idx: int, pos: int
660: ) -> int:
661:     nxt = idx + 1
662:     while nxt < len(parts) and parts[nxt][0] < pos:
663:         nxt += 1
664:     return nxt

667: def _parse_if(
668:     cur: _Cursor,
669:     base: int,
670:     stmt_e: int,
671:     parts: list[tuple[int, int]],
672:     idx: int,
673:     in_lambda: bool,

675:     text = cur.text
676:     stripped = text[base:stmt_e]

679:         cur.fail(

GR00-worktree\scripts\db_guard\structural_analysis\cfg.py
Read 1/1 file, 377 lines
1: """Conservative intraprocedural CFG construction (shadow-only).
2:
3: Builds one :class:`ControlFlowGraph` per SUPPORTED parsed callable body.
4: Edges are SYNTACTICALLY POSSIBLE paths, not proven execution: loops get
5: body/exit edges, every TRY gets conservative exceptional edges to each
6: CATCH, and a BARRIER_SCOPE's outgoing edge is UNKNOWN because lambda
7: execution is never assumed synchronous.  Dominance is NOT computed here —
8: that is GR-12's proof step.
9:
10: Mutation sites and barrier markers attach by exact span containment.  A site
11: that no modeled region contains is reported as
12: DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_MUTATION_SITE_UNRESOLVED and attached to ENTRY so it
13: stays reachable — never silently dropped.
14:
15: Construction raises ValueError on any internal invariant violation; the
16: caller (shadow report) classifies that as INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE.
17: """
18: from __future__ import annotations
19:
20: from .diagnostics import make_diagnostic
21: from .model import (
22:     EdgeKind,
23:     MutationSite,
24:     NodeKind,
25:     SourceSpan,
26:     StructuralDiagnostic,
27:     StructuralEdge,
28:     StructuralNode,
29:     BarrierMarker,
30:     ControlFlowGraph,
31: )
32: from .tokenizer import CallableBodyParse, RegionKind
33:
34: __all__ = ["build_callable_cfg"]
35:
36: _REGION_NODE_KIND = {
37:     RegionKind.STATEMENT: NodeKind.STATEMENT,
38:     RegionKind.IF: NodeKind.BRANCH,
39:     RegionKind.WHEN: NodeKind.WHEN,
40:     RegionKind.LOOP: NodeKind.LOOP_HEADER,
41:     RegionKind.TRY: NodeKind.TRY,
42:     RegionKind.CATCH: NodeKind.CATCH,
43:     RegionKind.FINALLY: NodeKind.FINALLY,
44:     RegionKind.RETURN: NodeKind.RETURN,
45:     RegionKind.THROW: NodeKind.THROW,
46:     RegionKind.BREAK: NodeKind.BREAK,
47:     RegionKind.CONTINUE: NodeKind.CONTINUE,
48:     RegionKind.ACCESSOR: NodeKind.STATEMENT,
49:     RegionKind.BARRIER_SCOPE: NodeKind.BARRIER_SCOPE,
50:     RegionKind.DIRECT_CHECK: NodeKind.BARRIER_CALL,
51: }
52:
53:
54: class _Builder:
55:     def __init__(self, text_len: int, body_span: SourceSpan) -> None:
56:         self.nodes: list[StructuralNode] = []
57:         self.edges: list[StructuralEdge] = []
58:         self.node_by_span: dict[tuple[int, int], str] = {}
59:         self.text_len = text_len
60:         self.body_span = body_span
61:         self.exit_normal: str | None = None
62:         self.exit_exceptional: str | None = None
63:         self._counter = 0
64:
65:     def add(self, kind: NodeKind, span: SourceSpan) -> str:
66:         self._counter += 1
67:         node_id = "n%03d" % self._counter
68:         self.nodes.append(StructuralNode(id=node_id, kind=kind, span=span))
69:         self.node_by_span.setdefault((span.start, span.end), node_id)
70:         return node_id
71:
72:     def edge(self, kind: EdgeKind, src: str, dst: str) -> None:
73:         self._counter += 1
74:         self.edges.append(
75:             StructuralEdge(
76:                 id="e%03d" % self._counter, kind=kind, source_node_id=src, target_node_id=dst
77:             )
78:         )
79:
80:     def span_at(self, offset: int) -> SourceSpan:
81:         return SourceSpan(start=offset, end=offset, line=1, column=1)
82:
83:     def ensure_exit_normal(self) -> str:
84:         if self.exit_normal is None:
85:             span = self.body_span
86:             self.exit_normal = self.add(
87:                 NodeKind.EXIT_NORMAL,
88:                 SourceSpan(
89:                     start=span.end, end=span.end, line=span.line, column=span.column
90:                 ),
91:             )
92:         return self.exit_normal
93:
94:     def ensure_exit_exceptional(self) -> str:
95:         if self.exit_exceptional is None:
96:             span = self.body_span
97:             self.exit_exceptional = self.add(
98:                 NodeKind.EXIT_EXCEPTIONAL,
99:                 SourceSpan(
100:                     start=span.end, end=span.end, line=span.line, column=span.column
101:                 ),
102:             )
103:         return self.exit_exceptional
104:
105:
106: def _innermost_owner(builder: _Builder, start: int, end: int) -> str | None:
107:     best: tuple[int, str] | None = None
108:     for (node_start, node_end), node_id in builder.node_by_span.items():
109:         if node_start <= start and end <= node_end:
110:             width = node_end - node_start
111:             if best is None or width < best[0]:
112:                 best = (width, node_id)
113:     return best[1] if best is not None else None
114:
115:
116: def _attach_site(
117:     builder: _Builder,
118:     start: int,
119:     end: int,
120:     kind: NodeKind,
121:     diagnostics: list[StructuralDiagnostic],
122:     unresolved_code: str,
123:     path: str,
124:     site_line: int,
125: ) -> None:
126:     owner = _innermost_owner(builder, start, end)
127:     if owner is None:
128:         diagnostics.append(
129:             make_diagnostic(unresolved_code, path, site_line)
130:         )
131:         owner = builder.nodes[0].id
132:     builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, owner, builder.add(kind, builder.span_at(start)))
133:
134:
135: def _build_regions(
136:     builder: _Builder,
137:     regions,
138:     in_lambda: bool,
139:     loop_ctx: dict | None,
140: ) -> tuple[list[str], list[str], list[tuple[EdgeKind, str]]]:
141:     """Build a statement sequence.
142:
143:     Returns (first_node_ids, last_normal_node_ids, dangling) where dangling
144:     holds (edge_kind, source_node_id) pairs that must connect to the sequence
145:     join (the next sibling's entry, the enclosing join, or the exit).
146:     """
147:     entries: list[tuple[list[str], list[str], list[tuple[EdgeKind, str]]]] = []
148:     for region in regions:
149:         entries.append(_build_region(builder, region, in_lambda, loop_ctx))
150:     non_empty = [
151:         item for item in entries if item[0] or item[2]
152:     ]
153:     firsts: list[str] = []
154:     lasts: list[str] = []
155:     dangling: list[tuple[EdgeKind, str]] = []
156:     for index, (region_firsts, region_lasts, region_dangling) in enumerate(non_empty):
157:         if index == 0:
158:             firsts.extend(region_firsts)
159:         else:
160:             for prev_last in non_empty[index - 1][1]:
161:                 for first in region_firsts:
162:                     builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, prev_last, first)
163:         if index == len(non_empty) - 1:
164:             lasts.extend(region_lasts)
165:             dangling.extend(region_dangling)
166:         else:
167:             for kind, src in region_dangling:
168:                 for first in non_empty[index + 1][0]:
169:                     builder.edge(kind, src, first)
170:     return firsts, lasts, dangling
171:
172:
173: def _build_region(
174:     builder: _Builder,
175:     region,
176:     in_lambda: bool,
177:     loop_ctx: dict | None,
178: ) -> tuple[list[str], list[str], list[tuple[EdgeKind, str]]]:
179:     kind = _REGION_NODE_KIND.get(region.kind)
180:     if kind is None:
181:         if region.kind in (RegionKind.BLOCK, RegionKind.WHEN_BRANCH):
182:             # Structural wrappers: the children carry the flow.
183:             return _build_regions(
184:                 builder, list(region.children), in_lambda, loop_ctx
185:             )
186:         return ([], [], [])
187:
188:     if region.kind == RegionKind.IF:
189:         branch = builder.add(kind, region.span)
190:         dangling: list[tuple[EdgeKind, str]] = []
191:         lasts: list[str] = []
192:         for child_index, child in enumerate(region.children):
193:             child_firsts, child_lasts, child_dangling = _build_regions(
194:                 builder, [child], in_lambda, loop_ctx
195:             )
196:             edge_kind = EdgeKind.TRUE_BRANCH if child_index == 0 else EdgeKind.FALSE_BRANCH
197:             for first in child_firsts:
198:                 builder.edge(edge_kind, branch, first)
199:             lasts.extend(child_lasts)
200:             dangling.extend(child_dangling)
201:         if len(region.children) < 2:
202:             dangling.append((EdgeKind.FALSE_BRANCH, branch))
203:         return ([branch], lasts, dangling)
204:
205:     if region.kind == RegionKind.WHEN:
206:         when_node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
207:         dangling = []
208:         lasts = []
209:         for child in region.children:
210:             child_firsts, child_lasts, child_dangling = _build_regions(
211:                 builder, [child], in_lambda, loop_ctx
212:             )
213:             for first in child_firsts:
214:                 builder.edge(EdgeKind.WHEN_BRANCH, when_node, first)
215:             lasts.extend(child_lasts)
216:             dangling.extend(child_dangling)
217:         return ([when_node], lasts, dangling)
218:
219:     if region.kind == RegionKind.LOOP:
220:         header = builder.add(kind, region.span)
221:         inner_ctx = {"header": header}
222:         body_firsts, body_lasts, body_dangling = _build_regions(
223:             builder, list(region.children), in_lambda, inner_ctx
224:         )
225:         for first in body_firsts:
226:             builder.edge(EdgeKind.LOOP_BODY, header, first)
227:         for last in body_lasts:
228:             builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, last, header)
229:         _ = body_dangling
230:         return ([header], [], [(EdgeKind.LOOP_EXIT, header)])
231:
232:     if region.kind == RegionKind.TRY:
233:         try_node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
234:         lasts: list[str] = []
235:         catch_nodes: list[str] = []
236:         finally_node: str | None = None
237:         body_firsts: list[str] = []
238:         for child in region.children:
239:             if child.kind == RegionKind.TRY:
240:                 body_firsts, body_lasts, body_dangling = _build_regions(
241:                     builder, list(child.children), in_lambda, loop_ctx
242:                 )
243:                 for first in body_firsts:
244:                     builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, try_node, first)
245:                 lasts.extend(body_lasts)
246:                 _ = body_dangling
247:             elif child.kind == RegionKind.CATCH:
248:                 catch_node = builder.add(NodeKind.CATCH, child.span)
249:                 catch_nodes.append(catch_node)
250:                 builder.edge(EdgeKind.EXCEPTION, try_node, catch_node)
251:                 catch_firsts, catch_lasts, catch_dangling = _build_regions(
252:                     builder, list(child.children), in_lambda, loop_ctx
253:                 )
254:                 for first in catch_firsts:
255:                     builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, catch_node, first)
256:                 lasts.extend(catch_lasts)
257:                 _ = catch_dangling
258:             elif child.kind == RegionKind.FINALLY:
259:                 finally_node = builder.add(NodeKind.FINALLY, child.span)
260:                 fin_firsts, fin_lasts, fin_dangling = _build_regions(
261:                     builder, list(child.children), in_lambda, loop_ctx
262:                 )
263:                 for first in fin_firsts:
264:                     builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, finally_node, first)
265:                 lasts.extend(fin_lasts)
266:                 _ = fin_dangling
267:         for node in [try_node] + catch_nodes:
268:             if finally_node is not None:
269:                 builder.edge(EdgeKind.FINALLY, node, finally_node)
270:         return ([try_node], lasts, [])
271:
272:     if region.kind == RegionKind.BARRIER_SCOPE:
273:         scope = builder.add(kind, region.span)
274:         scope_firsts, scope_lasts, scope_dangling = _build_regions(
275:             builder, list(region.children), True, loop_ctx
276:         )
277:         _ = scope_firsts
278:         for last in scope_lasts:
279:             builder.edge(EdgeKind.UNKNOWN, last, scope)
280:         return ([scope], [], [(EdgeKind.UNKNOWN, scope)] + scope_dangling)
281:
282:     if region.kind == RegionKind.BLOCK:
283:         return _build_regions(builder, list(region.children), in_lambda, loop_ctx)
284:
285:     if region.kind == RegionKind.RETURN:
286:         node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
287:         builder.edge(EdgeKind.RETURN_EXIT, node, builder.ensure_exit_normal())
288:         return ([node], [], [])
289:
290:     if region.kind == RegionKind.THROW:
291:         node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
292:         builder.edge(EdgeKind.THROW_EXIT, node, builder.ensure_exit_exceptional())
293:         return ([node], [], [])
294:
295:     if region.kind == RegionKind.BREAK:
296:         node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
297:         # The loop join is not materialized in GR-11; a break targets the
298:         # normal exit — a conservative over-approximation that adds no
299:         # entry->mutation path (GR-12 only reasons over those).
300:         builder.edge(EdgeKind.LOOP_EXIT, node, builder.ensure_exit_normal())
301:         return ([node], [], [])
302:
303:     if region.kind == RegionKind.CONTINUE:
304:         node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
305:         if loop_ctx is not None and "header" in loop_ctx:
306:             builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, node, loop_ctx["header"])
307:         return ([node], [], [])
308:
309:     node = builder.add(kind, region.span)
310:     return ([node], [node], [])
311:
312:
313: def build_callable_cfg(
314:     parse: CallableBodyParse,
315:     mutation_sites: tuple[MutationSite, ...] | list[MutationSite],
316:     barrier_markers: tuple[BarrierMarker, ...] | list[BarrierMarker],
317:     *,
318:     path: str,
319:     callable_key: str,
320: ) -> tuple[ControlFlowGraph, tuple[StructuralDiagnostic, ...]]:
321:     """Build the CFG for one SUPPORTED parsed callable body.
322:
323:     Returns (graph, diagnostics).  Raises ValueError when an internal graph
324:     invariant fails — the caller classifies that as INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE,
325:     never as an unsupported-source result.
326:     """
327:     if parse.unsupported:
328:         raise ValueError("cannot build a CFG for an unsupported callable body")
329:     builder = _Builder(0, parse.body_span)
330:     diagnostics: list[StructuralDiagnostic] = []
331:     entry = builder.add(
332:         NodeKind.ENTRY,
333:         SourceSpan(
334:             start=parse.body_span.start,
335:             end=parse.body_span.start,
336:             line=parse.body_span.line,
337:             column=parse.body_span.column,
338:         ),
339:     )
340:     firsts, lasts, dangling = _build_regions(builder, list(parse.regions), False, None)
341:     for first in firsts:
342:         builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, entry, first)
343:     exit_normal = builder.ensure_exit_normal()
344:     for last in lasts:
345:         builder.edge(EdgeKind.NORMAL, last, exit_normal)
346:     for edge_kind, source in dangling:
347:         builder.edge(edge_kind, source, exit_normal)
348:     for site in mutation_sites:
349:         _attach_site(
350:             builder,
351:             site.span.start,
352:             site.span.end,
353:             NodeKind.MUTATION,
354:             diagnostics,
355:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_MUTATION_SITE_UNRESOLVED",
356:             path,
357:             site.span.line,
358:         )
359:     for marker in barrier_markers:
360:         if (marker.span.start, marker.span.end) in builder.node_by_span:
361:             continue
362:         _attach_site(
363:             builder,
364:             marker.span.start,
365:             marker.span.end,
366:             NodeKind.BARRIER_CALL,
367:             diagnostics,
368:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BARRIER_FORM_UNRECOGNIZED",
369:             path,
370:             marker.span.line,
371:         )
372:     graph = ControlFlowGraph(
373:         callable_key=callable_key,
374:         nodes=tuple(builder.nodes),
375:         edges=tuple(builder.edges),
376:     )
377:     return graph, tuple(diagnostics)
Thinking
Critical observations from cfg.py:
1. **Mutation site attachment**: `_attach_site` finds the innermost region node whose span contains the site, then creates a MUTATION node with an edge FROM the owner. If no region contains it → diagnostic DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_MUTATION_SITE_UNRESOLVED + attached to ENTRY. For `return try {...}` under current behavior, the RETURN region spans the whole try body → mutation sites inside map to the RETURN node (innermost containing span) — with an edge RETURN→MUTATION. But wait — that MUTATION node would have NO path... it has an edge from the RETURN node. But the RETURN node exits to EXIT_NORMAL. So the mutation is reachable only via RETURN→MUTATION. Hmm, that's actually a path ENTRY→...→RETURN→MUTATION — a weird "mutation happens after return" model. It's conservative in the sense that the mutation IS reachable. OK.
2. **RETURN with children**: _build_region for RETURN (L285-288) ignores region.children — adds one node, exits. If I make `return try` emit a RETURN region with a TRY child, the try child would never be built because the RETURN handler doesn't recurse into children. I must extend the RETURN handler: when the region has children (the wrapped construct), build children too: RETURN node → NORMAL edge to child firsts; child lasts → RETURN node? Semantics: `return try {...} catch {...}`: the try body runs, then return exits. Model: RETURN node (span = whole statement) → NORMAL into try construct; the construct's lasts → edge to RETURN node? That creates a cycle RETURN→TRY→RETURN. Hmm.
Better model: for a returning construct, the construct's normal completion flows into the return exit. Since the RETURN node itself has the RETURN_EXIT edge, a cleaner approach: RETURN region with child → build child, connect: RETURN node --NORMAL--> child firsts; child lasts --NORMAL--> ... they need a join before the RETURN_EXIT. Simplest sound approach: make the RETURN node the *terminal* marker: edges: RETURN → child_firsts (NORMAL); child lasts → RETURN node (NORMAL)? cycle is ugly for dominance algorithms but dominator computation handles cycles fine.
Alternative cleaner: don't emit a separate RETURN node when there's a child construct; instead emit the child construct and connect child lasts → RETURN_EXIT directly. But then the "return" semantics (normal exit) is preserved: the construct's completion leads to exit. Model:
- RETURN-with-child: build child construct; child_firsts are the entry points (connect from previous statement's lasts); child lasts + dangling connect to... the RETURN region's lasts should be [] and its "exit" edges: for each child last, add RETURN_EXIT edge to exit_normal. Dangling edges (e.g., exceptional flows inside catch, UNKNOWN from barrier scopes) → also to exit_normal (as the sequence join does).
But _build_region's contract returns (firsts, lasts, dangling) — the caller connects lasts→next and dangling→join/exit. For RETURN I want: firsts = child_firsts, lasts = [], dangling = [(RETURN_EXIT, last) for each child last] + child_dangling? But dangling edges connect to exit_normal with the given edge kind — the caller at L346-347: `for edge_kind, source in dangling: builder.edge(edge_kind, source, exit_normal)`. So I can return dangling=[(EdgeKind.RETURN_EXIT, last) for last in child_lasts] + child_dangling. The child's exceptional/UNKNOWN danglings keep their kinds → they go to exit_normal... hmm, BARRIER_SCOPE dangling with UNKNOWN to exit_normal — matches existing semantics for a top-level scope (dangling connects to sequence join/exit).
But wait — the exceptional paths from try/catch: try's lasts already flow to exit; catch lasts also in child_lasts (both are "lasts" of the construct). Return semantics: whichever completes, we return. So RETURN_EXIT from both try-body lasts and catch lasts — correct.
This avoids a RETURN node entirely for construct returns. But then the region's node kind mapping — no RETURN node emitted. Does anything require a RETURN node? Dominance proof cares about paths; the plan's test matrix mentions "return before mutation" — RETURN node stops flow. For `return try`, the return is the last statement typically. The region span contains the whole statement so sites inside map to innermost children (try body statements), not the RETURN wrapper.
Hmm, but there's a subtlety: without a RETURN node, the `return` keyword itself isn't a node — fine, spans still cover.
Alternatively keep a RETURN node as a pass-through: RETURN node --NORMAL--> child_firsts, child lasts --NORMAL--> RETURN node, RETURN --RETURN_EXIT--> exit. Cycle RETURN↔TRY. Dominators: fine. But simpler graph without node is nicer. However — consistency: existing tests? No existing tests cover RETURN with children (didn't exist). I'll go with the pass-through-free model: RETURN with children → no RETURN node; construct flows directly, lasts become RETURN_EXIT danglings.
Wait, one more consideration: `return try` where try is NOT the last statement? `val x = return try...` invalid. `return try{...} catch{...}` is always a statement whose completion exits the callable. So lasts=[] is right.
Edge case: `return if (cond) a() else b()` — same: branch lasts → RETURN_EXIT.
But careful — the IF handler returns dangling (FALSE_BRANCH, branch) when no else — `return if (cond) a()` (no else): dangling FALSE_BRANCH from branch → exit_normal. The FALSE_BRANCH dangling gets connected to exit_normal with kind FALSE_BRANCH — sound (branch false → return anyway).
3. **RETURN without children** — unchanged: node + RETURN_EXIT.
Also `_REGION_NODE_KIND[RegionKind.RETURN] = NodeKind.RETURN`.
4. **CATCH children parse**: For dangling-clause callables, once the return-try parses, the try body statements (mutation `budgetDao.insert...` inside `when { }` branches etc.) — the when branches with unbraced RHS? addBudget's when: branches are `!x -> budgetDao.insert(budgetToInsert)` — unbraced RHS statements (calls). _parse_when branch RHS: rhs_s not starting with `{` → what happens? Let me look at the else path of _parse_when L882+ (didn't read past 882). Let me read the rest (883-905).
Also for PlannedExpenseRepository.addPlannedExpense, mutations inside... need to see the full function to be sure everything parses. And the earlier lambda-escape findings for addPlannedExpense were require/takeIf/emitPlannedDiagnostic — with opacity, become statements.
Let me read the tail of tokenizer (883-905, plus 1209-1370 for parse_callable_body + do-while splice) and shadow_report.py + parser.py quickly.
cfg.py's RETURN handler ignores children, so construct-returns need a tailored build. Let me read the tokenizer tail and the remaining pipeline files.

1220:     head_end = _match_forward(text, paren_abs, stmt_e)
1221:     if head_end < 0:
1222:         cur.fail(
1223:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
1224:             paren_abs,
1225:             stmt_e,
1226:             "unbalanced-paren",
1227:         )
1228:         return None
1229:     rest = text[head_end:stmt_e]
1230:     if not rest.strip(_WS).startswith("{"):
1231:         cur.fail(
1232:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
1233:             base,
1234:             stmt_e,
1235:             "accessor-without-braces",
1236:         )
1237:         return None
1238:     brace_abs = head_end + (len(rest) - len(rest.lstrip(_WS)))
1239:     close = _match_forward(text, brace_abs, stmt_e)
1240:     tail = text[close:stmt_e].strip(_WS) if close > 0 else ""
1241:     if close < 0 or tail:
1242:         cur.fail(
1243:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED"
1244:             if close > 0
1245:             else "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
1246:             base,
1247:             stmt_e,
1248:             "accessor-body-not-closed" if close > 0 else "unbalanced-brace",
1249:         )
1250:         return None
1251:     inner = _parse_sequence(cur, brace_abs + 1, close - 1, in_lambda)
1252:     return ParsedRegion(
1253:         kind=RegionKind.ACCESSOR, span=cur.span(base, close), children=tuple(inner)
1254:     )
1255:
1256:
1257: def parse_callable_body(masked_text: str, body_span: SourceSpan) -> CallableBodyParse:
1258:     if not isinstance(masked_text, str):
1259:         raise TypeError("masked_text must be a string")
1260:     if not isinstance(body_span, SourceSpan):
1261:         raise TypeError("body_span must be a SourceSpan")
1262:     cur = _Cursor(masked_text)
1263:     if '"' in masked_text or "'" in masked_text:
1264:         cur.fail(
1265:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
1266:             body_span.start,
1267:             body_span.end,
1268:             "masking-unverified",
1269:         )
1270:         return CallableBodyParse(
1271:             body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1272:         )
1273:     if (
1274:         body_span.start < 0
1275:         or body_span.end > len(masked_text)
1276:         or body_span.end < body_span.start
1277:     ):
1278:         cur.fail(
1279:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
1280:             0,
1281:             0,
1282:             "span-out-of-range",
1283:         )
1284:         return CallableBodyParse(
1285:             body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1286:         )
1287:     raw = masked_text[body_span.start : body_span.end]
1288:     if not raw.strip(_WS):
1289:         return CallableBodyParse(body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=())
1290:     lstripped = raw.lstrip(_WS)
1291:     if lstripped.startswith("=") and not lstripped.startswith("=="):
1292:         eq = body_span.start + (len(raw) - len(lstripped))
1293:         cur.fail(
1294:             "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
1295:             eq,
1296:             body_span.end,
1297:             "expression-body",
1298:         )
1299:         return CallableBodyParse(
1300:             body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1301:         )
1302:     content_start, content_end = body_span.start, body_span.end
1303:     if lstripped.startswith("{"):
1304:         open_abs = body_span.start + (len(raw) - len(lstripped))
1305:         close = _match_forward(masked_text, open_abs, body_span.end)
1306:         if close < 0:
1307:             cur.fail(
1308:                 "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_SYNTAX_UNBALANCED",
1309:                 open_abs,
1310:                 body_span.end,
1311:                 "unbalanced-brace",
1312:             )
1313:             return CallableBodyParse(
1314:                 body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1315:             )
1316:         if masked_text[close:body_span.end].strip(_WS):
1317:             cur.fail(
1318:                 "DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_BODY_UNSUPPORTED",
1319:                 open_abs,
1320:                 body_span.end,
1321:                 "body-trailing-content",
1322:             )
1323:             return CallableBodyParse(
1324:                 body_span=body_span, regions=(), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1325:             )
1326:         content_start, content_end = open_abs + 1, close - 1
1327:     regions = _parse_sequence(cur, content_start, content_end, False)
1328:     regions = _splice_dowhile(cur, masked_text, body_span, regions)
1329:     return CallableBodyParse(
1330:         body_span=body_span, regions=tuple(regions), unsupported=tuple(cur.findings)
1331:     )
1332:
1333:
1334: def _splice_dowhile(
1335:     cur: _Cursor,
1336:     text: str,
1337:     body_span: SourceSpan,
1338:     regions: list[ParsedRegion],
1339: ) -> list[ParsedRegion]:
1340:     _ = (cur, text, body_span)
1341:     out: list[ParsedRegion] = []
1342:     skip_next_statement = False
1343:     for region in regions:
1344:         if skip_next_statement and region.kind == RegionKind.STATEMENT:
1345:             inner_text = text[region.span.start : region.span.end]
1346:             if _leading_kw(_strip(inner_text), "while"):
1347:                 skip_next_statement = False
1348:                 continue
1349:             skip_next_statement = False
1350:         if isinstance(region, _DoWhile):
1351:             skip_next_statement = True
1352:             plain = ParsedRegion(
1353:                 kind=region.kind, span=region.span, children=region.children
1354:             )
1355:             out.append(plain)
1356:             continue
1357:         if region.children:
1358:             fixed_children = _splice_dowhile(cur, text, body_span, list(region.children))
1359:             if fixed_children != list(region.children):
1360:                 out.append(

1363:                         span=region.span,
1364:                         children=tuple(fixed_children),
1365:                         barrier=region.barrier,
1366:                     )
1367:                 )
1368:                 continue
1369:         out.append(region)
1370:     return out

1: """Deterministic shadow-report assembly (shadow-only).
2:
3: Turns per-callable structural analyses into the report dict defined in

5: to exactly one of SUPPORTED, UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY, or
6: INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE; a CFG construction failure is infrastructure, never
7: "unsupported source".  The report carries bounded coordinates only (path,
8: line) — never raw source, offsets, SQL, exception text, or absolute paths.
9: """

98: def _result_entry(result: StructuralAnalysisResult, include_graph: bool) -> dict:

101:         "status": result.status.value,
102:         "nodeCount": result.node_count,

144:                 }

151: def build_shadow_report(
152:     results,
153:     target_sha: str | None = None,
154:     include_graphs: bool = False,
155: ) -> dict:
156:     """Assemble the shadow report dict from StructuralAnalysisResult objects.
157:
158:     Deterministic: callables sorted by callableKey; diagnostics already
159:     sorted by the model.  ``include_graphs`` adds the node/edge payloads for
160:     GR-12 consumption; the default CLI report omits them.
161:     """
162:     ordered = sorted(results, key=lambda item: item.callable_key)
163:     supported = sum(1 for item in ordered if item.status is AnalysisStatus.SUPPORTED)
164:     unsupported = sum(
165:         1 for item in ordered if item.status is AnalysisStatus.UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY
166:     )
167:     infrastructure = sum(
168:         1 for item in ordered if item.status is AnalysisStatus.INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE
169:     )
170:     return {
171:         "schemaVersion": _REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
172:         "reportOnly": True,
173:         "targetSha": target_sha,
174:         "summary": {
175:             "callableCount": len(ordered),
176:             "supportedCount": supported,
177:             "unsupportedCount": unsupported,
178:             "infrastructureFailureCount": infrastructure,
179:         },
180:         "callables": [_result_entry(item, include_graphs) for item in ordered],
181:     }

Read 1/1 file, 94 lines
1: """Per-callable classification over the conservative tokenizer (shadow-only).
2:
3: Turns one :class:`CallableBodyParse` into the GR-11 structural verdict for a
4: callable: SUPPORTED with the syntax families its body used, or
5: UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY with one closed-code diagnostic per conservative
6: stop.  No dominance, mediation, or safety claim is produced here — that is
7: GR-12/GR-13.  Diagnostics carry bounded coordinates only (path, line,
8: callable key, syntax family); raw source text never enters this layer.
9: """
10: from __future__ import annotations
11:
12: from dataclasses import dataclass
13:
14: from .diagnostics import make_diagnostic
15: from .model import AnalysisStatus, SourceSpan, StructuralDiagnostic, SyntaxFamily
16: from .tokenizer import CallableBodyParse, RegionKind, parse_callable_body
17:
18: __all__ = [
19:     "CallableBodyClassification",
20:     "classify_callable_body",
21:     "analyze_callable_body",
22: ]
23:
24: _FAMILIES_BY_KIND = {
25:     RegionKind.IF: SyntaxFamily.IF_ELSE,
26:     RegionKind.WHEN: SyntaxFamily.WHEN,
27:     RegionKind.LOOP: SyntaxFamily.LOOP,
28:     RegionKind.TRY: SyntaxFamily.TRY_FINALLY,
29:     RegionKind.CATCH: SyntaxFamily.TRY_FINALLY,
30:     RegionKind.FINALLY: SyntaxFamily.TRY_FINALLY,
31:     RegionKind.BARRIER_SCOPE: SyntaxFamily.NESTED_LAMBDA,
32: }
33:
34:
35: @dataclass(frozen=True)
36: class CallableBodyClassification:
37:     """Conservative structural verdict for one callable body."""
38:
39:     status: AnalysisStatus
40:     syntax_families: tuple[SyntaxFamily, ...]
41:     diagnostics: tuple[StructuralDiagnostic, ...]
42:
43:
44: def _region_families(parse: CallableBodyParse) -> tuple[SyntaxFamily, ...]:
45:     families: set[SyntaxFamily] = set()
46:
47:     def walk(regions) -> None:
48:         for region in regions:
49:             family = _FAMILIES_BY_KIND.get(region.kind)
50:             if family is not None:
51:                 families.add(family)
52:             walk(region.children)
53:
54:     walk(parse.regions)
55:     return tuple(sorted(families, key=lambda item: item.value))
56:
57:
58: def classify_callable_body(
59:     parse: CallableBodyParse, *, path: str, callable_key: str
60: ) -> CallableBodyClassification:
61:     """Classify one tokenizer result for an exact callable identity."""
62:     if parse.unsupported:
63:         diagnostics = tuple(
64:             make_diagnostic(
65:                 finding.code,
66:                 path,
67:                 finding.span.line,
68:                 callable_key=callable_key,
69:                 syntax_family=SyntaxFamily.UNKNOWN_CONSTRUCT,
70:             )
71:             for finding in parse.unsupported
72:         )
73:         return CallableBodyClassification(
74:             status=AnalysisStatus.UNSUPPORTED_CONSERVATIVELY,
75:             syntax_families=(),
76:             diagnostics=diagnostics,
77:         )
78:     return CallableBodyClassification(
79:         status=AnalysisStatus.SUPPORTED,
80:         syntax_families=_region_families(parse),
81:         diagnostics=(),
82:     )
83:
84:
85: def analyze_callable_body(
86:     masked_text: str,
87:     body_span: SourceSpan,
88:     *,
89:     path: str,
90:     callable_key: str,
91: ) -> CallableBodyClassification:
92:     """Parse and classify one callable body span in one step."""
93:     parse = parse_callable_body(masked_text, body_span)
94:     return classify_callable_body(parse, path=path, callable_key=callable_key)

Both parse the same way — doSomething is the body; otherStatement is next. That's correct Kotlin semantics! The unbraced if body is exactly ONE statement. So consuming exactly one next part as body is correct.
BUT there's a risk:
```kotlin

someStatement()
```
One-line: rest = `return` → body = RETURN region. Good.

Edge: `if (cond) { } else { }` where the else is on the next part — existing loop handles.

Hmm, offsets get fiddly. Let me define: body chunk = text from cond_end (skipping ws) to (else_pos_abs if else found else stmt_e). Parse sequence over that span; require non-empty regions (else fail "if-without-body").

If I bind to outer: then-body = `if (b) x()`, else = `y()` — semantically different model! How much does this matter? For soundness of dominance: the difference is which branch y() goes in. The mutation/barrier dominance could differ. Conservative approach: if the then-body chunk contains an `if` keyword (nested if), REFUSE to bind the trailing else to the outer if — fail closed "ambiguous-unbraced-else". Handling: when else found at top level of rest, parse then-chunk as sequence; if the LAST region of then-chunk is an IF region without else that could take it... complex.
Pragmatic fail-closed rule: if then-chunk's top-level parse yields an IF region whose span ends within 0 chars of the else AND ... no. Simplest sound rule: if the then-chunk contains any top-level `if` region, fail closed on the outer else binding ("unbraced-else-binding-ambiguous") — UNLESS the nested if already consumed the else... it can't, the else is outside the then-chunk by construction.

The trailing `else y()` then gets attached by MY code to the outer if — wrong per Kotlin binding. Fail closed when the then-chunk's last top-level region is an IF (possible dangling else). That's conservative and simple: "ambiguous-dangling-else" failure.

### Edit 3 — opacity predicate
Signature: parse_callable_body(masked_text, body_span, *, lambda_opacity_predicate=None). In _parse_sequence at L643 (the `{" in stripped or "}" in stripped` case):
```python

```
Wait — need care: `}` in stripped without `{`? e.g. `foo(})`? Unbalanced — predicate should handle. Keep: call predicate(base, stmt_e); if True → STATEMENT region; else fail lambda-escape as today.
Hmm, but should the opacity check apply only when the braces are lambda-ish? The predicate decides. In shadow_report, build the predicate:
```python
def _make_lambda_opacity_predicate(masked_text, mutation_sites, marker_spans):
site_starts = tuple(sorted(site.span.start for site in mutation_sites))
marker_spans = tuple((m.span.start, m.span.end) for m in markers)
def predicate(start, end):
# every top-level brace group within [start, end) must contain no site start and no marker span
for (gs, ge) in _top_level_brace_groups(masked_text, start, end):
if any(gs <= s < ge for s in site_starts): return False
if any(gs <= ms < ge or gs < me <= ge for ms, me in marker_spans): return False
return True
return predicate
```
Where do markers come from before parse? Regex-based barrier-like spans: scan masked_text[body_span] with _RE_BARRIER_SCOPE/_RE_BARRIER_CHECK/_RE_WORKER_GUARD/_RE_LIKE_BARRIER from tokenizer — but those are private. I'll add a small public function in barrier_markers.py: `barrier_like_call_spans(masked_text, start, end) -> tuple[tuple[int,int], ...]` using the regexes (import from tokenizer — they're module-level, importable). Also canonical receiver shapes are covered by _RE_BARRIER_SCOPE/CHECK.
_top_level_brace_groups: scan [start,end) tracking depth of (){}[]; a `{` at depth 0 (relative to the scan) starts a group until its match. Note: for `require(x) { ... }` the `{` is at depth 0 after the call parens. For `insertOrUpdateAll(rates.map { it.toEntity() })` the `{` is INSIDE parens (depth 1). Hmm! The lambda is nested inside the call's parens. So brace-group scan must find `{...}` groups at ANY depth, but only lambda-ish ones? Simpler: find ALL `{...}` groups in the statement span (each matched pair, non-overlapping, at any nesting), check each for site/marker containment. Any `{` group that contains a site start or marker → fail. That's the actual soundness rule: no hidden mutation/barrier in any lambda body.
But WAIT — a subtle problem: what about `{` groups that are NOT lambdas — e.g. in `when {`? when is handled earlier in dispatch. In a leaf statement, `{` groups are lambdas (or anonymous objects — handled earlier at L380). Class literals `Foo::class`? No braces. `mapOf<String, Int>()`? no braces. String templates? Masked. So all `{` groups in leaf statements are lambda bodies.
What about the statement being `val x = if (a) { y() } else { z() }`? But they're not lambdas! `val x = if (a) { y() } else { z() }` — if-expression with block branches. Modeling as lambda-escape is conservative (current behavior unchanged). ```python
_BARRIER_LIKE_SPAN_RE = re.compile(r"\b\w+\s*\.\s*(?:runWrite|checkWritesAllowed)\s*[\(\{]")
# reuse tokenizer's worker-guard regex too
def barrier_like_call_spans(masked_text, start, end):

Brace groups helper — put in barrier_markers.py too (it needs it for predicate) or tokenizer? The predicate factory will live in shadow_report or barrier_markers. I'll put both helpers in barrier_markers.py (it's the marker/observation module):
```python
def _top_level_brace_groups(text, start, end):
groups = []
i = start
while i < end:
ch = text[i]
if ch in "([{":
close = _match_forward(text, i, end)
if ch == "{":
if close < 0:
return None  # unbalanced → caller fails closed
groups.append((i, close))
if close < 0:
return None
i = close
continue
i += 1
return tuple(groups)
```
Hmm — this skips only the matched group of a `{` but for `(` it jumps to close — skipping possible inner `{` of lambdas inside parens? `foo(x.map { it })`: `(` at i → jump to `)` close — inner `{ }` skipped! That's WRONG for our containment check — the lambda inside parens wouldn't be checked. Fix: only jump for `{`; for `(`/`[` just increment (they don't hide braces; braces inside parens still get found by linear scan). Actually simpler: linear scan for every `{`, match forward, group, continue after close:
```python
def _brace_groups(text, start, end):
groups = []
i = start
while i < end:
if text[i] == "{":
close = _match_forward(text, i, end)
if close < 0:
return None
groups.append((i, close))
i = close
else:
i += 1
return tuple(groups)
```

Predicate in shadow_report:
```python
def _lambda_opacity_predicate(masked_text, body_span, mutation_sites, barrier_like_spans):
site_starts = tuple(sorted(site.span.start for site in mutation_sites))
marker_spans = tuple(barrier_like_spans)
def predicate(start, end):
groups = _brace_groups(masked_text, start, end)
if groups is None:
return False
for gs, ge in groups:
if any(gs <= s < ge for s in site_starts):
return False
if any(gs <= ms and me <= ge for ms, me in marker_spans):
return False
return True
return predicate
```
Wait — should the predicate consider barrier-like spans that OVERLAP the group but start before? A marker call `foo.runWrite(` starts before the `{` — the span end includes `[\(\{]` char only (regex end at the paren/brace char). `foo.runWrite {` span ends at `{`. Group starts AT `{`. `gs <= ms and me <= ge`: ms < gs → not contained → predicate True → opaque statement swallowing a runWrite scope!? DANGER. Statement `foo.runWrite { ... }` — but wait, that would be caught by _RE_LIKE_BARRIER at dispatch L600 (barrier-form-unrecognized) BEFORE reaching L643. And `writeBarrier.runWrite {` → BARRIER_SCOPE branch earlier. So statements reaching L643 with braces are non-barrier-call statements. But defense-in-depth: use OVERLAP for markers: `ms < ge and me > gs`. With `foo.runWrite {`: marker span (ms, me={pos+1}); group (gs={pos}, ge=close+1). overlap: ms < ge and me > gs → True → not opaque. Good, use overlap.
Also mutation site OVERLAP vs START? Site span = the DAO call span. If a site starts inside the lambda → flag. Could a site span overlap a lambda group without starting inside? Site = `exchangeRateDao.insertOrUpdateAll(rates.map { it.toEntity() })` — the SITE span covers the whole call including the lambda! Site start = at `exchangeRateDao` — BEFORE the group. Overlap test would flag the group → lambda-escape preserved → insertOrUpdateAll stays UNSUPPORTED. BAD!
So for sites use START containment (site start inside lambda body = mutation call textually inside lambda), not overlap. But the insertOrUpdateAll case: the site's call span CONTAINS the lambda group. Check "site start in group" → `gs <= s < ge`: s = start of `exchangeRateDao...` < gs → not in group → opaque. The mutation node then = the STATEMENT node (site attaches via innermost containing span = the opaque statement).
But subtle: `foo { dao.insert(x) }` — site start inside group → flagged → lambda-escape. Correct.
Mixed: `val x = when { a -> dao.insert(...) }` — val-construct path parses the when; sites inside branches — fine.
What about nested groups: `foo(bar { dao.insert(x) })` — inner group contains site start → flagged. Good.
Now the "guarded spans"... also need: barrier MARKERS from the parse (region-derived) — not needed for the predicate since region-derived markers come from recognized regions, which are parsed instead of being opaque. Regex spans cover all call-shaped barriers. Good.

296	def _leading_kw(stmt: str, word: str) -> bool:
297	    if not stmt.startswith(word):
298	        return False
299	    rest = stmt[len(word) :]
300	    return not rest or not (rest[0].isalnum() or rest[0] == "_")
301
302
303	def _find_top_level_else(text: str, start: int, end: int) -> int | None:
304	    """Absolute offset of a depth-0 ``else`` keyword in ``[start, end)``, or
305	    None.  Nesting depth counts ``( )``, ``[ ]`` and ``{ }``; masked text
306	    contains no comments or strings."""
307	    depth = 0
308	    i = start
309	    while i < end:
310	        ch = text[i]
311	        if ch in "([{":
312	            depth += 1
313	        elif ch in ")]}":
314	            depth -= 1
315	        elif depth == 0 and ch == "e" and text.startswith("else", i):
316	            before_ok = i == start or not (text[i - 1].isalnum() or text[i - 1] == "_")
317	            after = i + 4
318	            after_ok = after >= end or not (text[after].isalnum() or text[after] == "_")
319	            if before_ok and after_ok:
320	                return i
321	        i += 1
322	    return None

132	def barrier_like_call_spans(
133	    masked_text: str, start: int, end: int
134	) -> tuple[tuple[int, int], ...]:
135	    """Spans of any barrier-shaped call site inside ``[start, end)``.
136
137	    Parse-independent (regex over masked text): canonical
138	    ``writeBarrier.runWrite {`` / ``writeBarrier.checkWritesAllowed(``
139	    receivers, any-receiver ``runWrite`` / ``checkWritesAllowed`` shapes, and
140	    worker-guard scopes.  Used by the opaque-lambda soundness gate so a
141	    lambda body is never modeled opaque when it hides a barrier call.
142	    """
143	    spans: set[tuple[int, int]] = set()
144	    for pattern in (_RE_LIKE_BARRIER, _RE_WORKER_GUARD):
145	        for match in pattern.finditer(masked_text, start, end):
146	            spans.add((match.start(), match.end()))
147	    return tuple(sorted(spans))
148
149

151	    text: str, start: int, end: int
152	) -> tuple[tuple[int, int], ...] | None:
153	    """Matched ``{ ... }`` groups inside ``[start, end)``, or None when a
154	    brace never closes."""
155	    groups: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
156	    i = start
157	    while i < end:
158	        if text[i] == "{":
159	            close = _match_forward(text, i, end)
160	            if close < 0:

48	def analyze_callable_structurally(
49	    masked_text: str,
50	    body_span,
51	    path: str,
52	    callable_key: str,
53	    mutation_sites=(),

57	    """Run parse -> markers -> CFG for one callable and return the result.
58
59	    ``barrier_marker_fn(parse, masked_text) -> tuple[BarrierMarker, ...]`` is
60	    injected so this module stays decoupled from marker extraction.
61	    ``opacity_predicate_fn`` overrides the opaque-lambda soundness gate; by
62	    default the gate is active only when mutation sites are supplied (the
63	    analyzer knows what must never hide inside a lambda body).
64	    """
65	    opacity = opacity_predicate_fn
66	    if opacity is None and mutation_sites:
67	        opacity = _default_opacity_predicate(masked_text, body_span, mutation_sites)
68	    try:

70	            masked_text, body_span, lambda_opacity_predicate=opacity

996	) -> ParsedRegion | None:
997	    text = cur.text
998	    stripped = text[base:stmt_e]

1000	    kw_end = base + 4

60:      * Atomically inserts a new overall budget and deactivates any previously
61:      * active overall budget.
62:      */
63:     @Transaction
64:     suspend fun insertAndActivateOverall(budget: Budget): Long {
65:         require(budget.isActive) {
66:             "insertAndActivateOverall requires an active budget"
67:         }
68:         // Deactivate every currently-active overall budget (id 0 matches none
69:         // during the deactivation pass; the real keepId is applied after insert).
70:         @Suppress("KotlinConstantConditions")
71:         deactivateAllActiveOverallBudgets()
72:         return insert(budget.copy(
73:             activeOverallKey = 1,
74:             activeCategoryKey = null
75:         ))
76:     }
77:
78:     /**
79:      * Atomically inserts a new category budget and deactivates any previously
80:      * active budget for the same [Budget.categoryId].
81:      */
82:     @Transaction

84:         require(budget.isActive) {
85:             "insertAndActivateCategory requires an active budget"
86:         }
87:         val catId = requireNotNull(budget.categoryId) {
88:             "insertAndActivateCategory requires a non-null categoryId"
89:         }
90:         deactivateAllActiveCategoryBudgets(catId)
91:         return insert(budget.copy(
92:             activeOverallKey = null,
93:             activeCategoryKey = catId
94:         ))
95:     }

REGRESSION cause.
Fix: in the val-construct handler, if the construct parse fails/produces no region, fall back to the old behavior (plain STATEMENT) instead of failing? Careful — that would mask real failures: `val x = when { dao.insert() }` falling back to plain STATEMENT would HIDE mutations inside the when braces as a plain statement — the statement node's span covers the sites, so sites attach to the statement node (innermost containing span = the statement). 515	                    elif construct == "if":
516	                        region, consumed = _parse_if(
517	                            cur, cons_base, stmt_e, parts, idx, in_lambda
518	                        )
519	                    else:
520	                        region = _parse_when(cur, cons_base, stmt_e, in_lambda)
521	                        consumed = idx + 1
522	                    if region is not None:
523	                        out.append(
524	                            ParsedRegion(
525	                                kind=RegionKind.RETURN,
526	                                span=cur.span(base, region.span.end),
527	                                children=(region,),
528	                            )
529	                        )
530	                        idx = consumed
531	                        continue
532	                    # Construct parse failed.  When an opacity gate proves the
533	                    # statement hides no mutation site and no barrier-like
534	                    # call, roll the findings back and keep the historical
535	                    # leaf-RETURN model (sound: nothing is hidden).  With no
536	                    # gate, keep the historical leaf too.  Otherwise fail
537	                    # closed on the construct findings.
538	                    construct_findings = cur.findings[findings_before:]
539	                    del cur.findings[findings_before:]
540	                    if (
541	                        cur.opacity_predicate is not None
542	                        and not cur.opacity_predicate(base, stmt_e)
543	                    ):
544	                        cur.findings.extend(construct_findings)

16: def site_at(source: str, needle: str) -> MutationSite:
17:     start = source.index(needle)
18:     return MutationSite(
19:         span=SourceSpan(
20:             start=start,
21:             end=start + len(needle),
22:             line=source.count("\n", 0, start) + 1,
23:             column=start - source.rfind("\n", 0, start),
24:         ),
25:         callable_key="p|o|function|m|null|",
26:         dao_fqcn="example.Dao",
27:         operation="insert",
28:         mutation_kind="ROOM_ABSTRACT_INSERT",
29:         source_identity="example.Dao::insert",
30:     )
31:
32:
33: def build(source: str, sites=()):
34:     return build_callable_cfg(
35:         parse(source),
36:         tuple(sites),
37:         (),
38:         path="app/src/main/java/A.kt",
39:         callable_key="p|o|function|m|null|",
40:     )
41:
42:
43: def nodes_of(graph, kind: NodeKind):
44:     return [node for node in graph.nodes if node.kind is kind]
45:
46:
47: def edge_pairs(graph, kind: EdgeKind | None = None):
48:     return [
49:         (edge.source_node_id, edge.target_node_id, edge.kind)
50:         for edge in graph.edges
51:         if kind is None or edge.kind is kind
52:     ]
53:
54:
55: def successors(graph, node_id: str):
56:     return [
57:         (edge.target_node_id, edge.kind)
58:         for edge in graph.edges
59:         if edge.source_node_id == node_id
60:     ]
61:

Now append CFG tests for the new constructs. I need a `build` variant with predicate for the opaque-lambda CFG tests — the existing `build` uses `parse()` (no predicate). I'll add a helper `build_with_predicate(source, sites, site_starts=(), barrier_spans=())` — actually simpler: add a `build_opaque(source, sites)` that builds the gate from the sites themselves (mirroring shadow_report's default): use lambda_opacity_predicate with site spans + barrier_like_call_spans. Let me write:
```python
def build_gated(source: str, sites=()):
from scripts.db_guard.structural_analysis.barrier_markers import (
barrier_like_call_spans, lambda_opacity_predicate,
)
from scripts.kotlin_callable_parser import mask_kotlin_source
from scripts.db_guard.structural_analysis.tokenizer import parse_callable_body
from scripts.db_guard.structural_analysis.model import SourceSpan as SS
masked = mask_kotlin_source(source)
predicate = lambda_opacity_predicate(
masked, tuple(sites), barrier_like_call_spans(masked, 0, len(masked))
)
parse_result = parse_callable_body(masked, SS(0, len(masked), 1, 1), lambda_opacity_predicate=predicate)
return build_callable_cfg(parse_result, tuple(sites), (), path="app/src/main/java/A.kt", callable_key="p|o|function|m|null|")
```
CFG tests:
1. return-try-catch: mutation in try body; mutation reachable; EXCEPTION edge try→catch; mutation in catch body has path via catch node.
2. return-if-else unbraced: mutation in then branch → TRUE_BRANCH from branch node to mutation's statement.
3. val-when: mutations in branches map to MUTATION nodes inside WHEN_BRANCH flow.
4. opaque lambda: `exchangeRateDao.insertOrUpdateAll(rates.map { it.toEntity() })` with site → MUTATION node exists, diagnostics == ().
5. site inside lambda with gate → build_callable_cfg raises ValueError (unsupported parse) — actually build() raises since parse.unsupported. Test: pytest.raises(ValueError) on build_gated with site inside lambda.
6. mutation-free lambda statement with gate: node exists, site AFTER it still maps (sequential flow: statement → mutation).

Note: showing lines 141-215. More of the file remains. Use offset=216 to continue.
141: ## Supported Kotlin subset
142:
143: The initial model supports, where present in the corpus (no pretense of
144: parsing all Kotlin):
145:
146: ```text
147: braced functions
148: sequential statements
149: nested blocks
150: if / else
151: when with explicit branches
152: while / for / do-while
153: try / catch / finally
154: return
155: throw
156: break
157: continue
158: property getters/setters with braced bodies
159: simple direct barrier calls
160: simple DAO mutation sites supplied by D4
161: ```
162:
163: ## Unsupported constructs
164:
165: The following may be **represented**, but must not yield any future proof
166: until explicitly supported:
167:
168: ```text
169: expression bodies
170: local functions
171: anonymous objects
172: coroutines
173: launch / async
174: callbacks
175: escaping lambdas
176: unknown higher-order functions
177: reflection
178: dynamic dispatch
179: exception type filtering
180: inline/reified behavior
181: non-local returns
182: labelled returns
183: complex Elvis/short-circuit control flow
184: ```
185:
186: Each unsupported form emits one controlled diagnostic/result. Unsupported
187: syntax is never silently flattened into sequential statements.
188:
189: ## Lambda and callback treatment
190:
191: - A lambda is **not assumed to run synchronously**.
192: - Only a later, explicitly reviewed contract may treat an exact known wrapper
193:   as synchronous. That decision is **deferred** — it is not made in GR-11.
194: - `writeBarrier.runWrite { ... }` may be recognized as a `BARRIER_SCOPE`
195:   **syntactic candidate**. No proof result is emitted for it in GR-11.
196: - Any unrecognized lambda / callback / coroutine boundary preserves a
197:   `LAMBDA_DEFERRED` or `UNKNOWN` edge.
198: - A barrier inside a sibling lambda or local function is **not** attached to
199:   the mutation's path.
200:
201: ## Exception-flow treatment
202:
203: - `try` / `catch` / `finally` are modeled structurally (`TRY` / `CATCH` /
204:   `FINALLY` nodes; `EXCEPTION` / `FINALLY` / `THROW_EXIT` edges) where the
205:   syntax is supported.
206: - The model **never assumes runtime exception behavior**: which exception
207:   fires, whether `finally` runs relative to a mutation, or whether a `catch`
208:   filters by type. Exception type filtering is unsupported and yields a
209:   controlled diagnostic (`DB_STRUCTURAL_MODEL_EXCEPTION_FLOW_UNSUPPORTED`).
210: - A barrier observed in `catch` or `finally` is recorded at its own span on
211:   its own path — it is never normalized into the fall-through path of the
212:   `try` body.
213: - Dominance across exceptional edges is a GR-12 decision, not a GR-11 result.
214:
215: ## Barrier observation vocabulary

Keep it minimal and precise.

Snippet (lines 143-182 of 508):
143	The initial model supports, where present in the corpus (no pretense of
144	parsing all Kotlin):
145
146	```text
147	braced functions
148	sequential statements
149	nested blocks
150	if / else (braced or single-statement unbraced bodies)
151	when with explicit branches (with or without subject)
152	while / for / do-while
153	try / catch / finally
154	return (plain, and `return try/if/when ...` construct returns)
155	throw
156	break
157	continue
158	property getters/setters with braced bodies
159	`val`/`var` declarations whose initializer is an if/when/try construct
160	simple direct barrier calls
161	simple DAO mutation sites supplied by D4
162	```
163
164	### GR-12 capability extension (soundness rules)
165
166	PR-GR-12 extended the GR-11 tokenizer/CFG without changing the closed
167	vocabularies. Three conservative rejections were relaxed under the following
168	fail-closed rules (enforced by tests in
169	`scripts/db_guard/structural_analysis/`):
170
171	1. **`return try/if/when ...` and `val/var x = if/when/try ...`** — the
172	   wrapped construct is parsed as the region's child and carries the flow
173	   (`RETURN` with children exits via its construct's normal completion).
174	   When the construct parse fails, the statement may fall back to the
175	   historical leaf model ONLY when the opaque-lambda gate (below) proves the
176	   statement hides no mutation site and no barrier-like call; otherwise the
177	   construct's conservative failure stands.
178	2. **Unbraced single-statement if/else bodies** — the body is exactly one
179	   parsed statement (or the next statement part when the header ends the
180	   line). A trailing `else` after a nested unbraced `if` fails closed
181	   (`ambiguous-dangling-else`: Kotlin binds it to the nearest `if`, and
182	   misplacing branch flow would be unsound).

214	Each unsupported form emits one controlled diagnostic/result. Unsupported
215	syntax is never silently flattened into sequential statements.

217	## Lambda and callback treatment
218
219	- A lambda is **not assumed to run synchronously**.
220	- Only a later, explicitly reviewed contract may treat an exact known wrapper
221	  as synchronous. That decision is **deferred** — it is not made in GR-11.
222	- `writeBarrier.runWrite { ... }` may be recognized as a `BARRIER_SCOPE`
223	  **syntactic candidate**. No proof result is emitted for it in GR-11.
224	- Any unrecognized lambda / callback / coroutine boundary preserves a
225	  `LAMBDA_DEFERRED` or `UNKNOWN` edge.
226	- A barrier inside a sibling lambda or local function is **not** attached to
227	  the mutation's path.
228	- GR-12 opaque-lambda modeling: a lambda body that provably contains no
229	  mutation site and no barrier-like call may be collapsed into its enclosing
230	  statement node (nothing relevant is hidden, so no synchronicity assumption
231	  is needed). Any lambda that hides a mutation site or barrier-shaped call
232	  keeps the strict conservative failure — see the GR-12 capability extension
233	  rules above.
234
235	## Exception-flow treatment
