Thinking
I have the model. Now design the proof module. Key components:
### 1. CanonicalBarrierContract (typed, code-owned)
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CanonicalBarrierContract:
receiver_fqcn: str
direct_check_methods: tuple[str, ...]  # ("checkWritesAllowed",)
guarded_scope_methods: tuple[str, ...]  # ("runWrite",)
contract_version: int
```
With validation: no wildcards, FQCN must contain a dot. Version 1 pins the facts from GR-12_CANONICAL_BARRIER_API.md.
### 2. Receiver type resolution (exact)
For a barrier-like call `writeBarrier.checkWritesAllowed(...)` inside a callable in file F, class C:
- receiver simple name = `writeBarrier`
- Resolve: find the property declaration in F: `val writeBarrier: DatabaseWriteBarrier` (or `private val writeBarrier: DatabaseWriteBarrier`). Extract declared type; qualify to FQCN using the file's import table (`import com.yourname.expensetracker.data.backup.DatabaseWriteBarrier`) or same-package assumption.
- If the receiver name has NO declared property in the file → unresolved → exit-2 style diagnostic (DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_RECEIVER_UNRESOLVED).
- If declared type is not the canonical FQCN → not a barrier.
Implementation over masked text: parse the file header (package + imports) and the class body property declarations. Scope: only needs to resolve receivers that look like barrier calls (from barrier markers). Steps:
1. package line: `package com.yourname.expensetracker.data.backup` → package FQCN.
2. imports: map simple name → FQCN.
3. property declarations within the file: regex `\b(?:val|var)\s+(\w+)\s*:\s*([\w.<>?]+)` — captures name + type. For the receiver name, get declared type simple name → FQCN = import map or package + "." + simple.
4. Ambiguity: multiple declarations of the same name → unresolved (exit 2). None → unresolved.
Also parameters/constructor params: `writeBarrier` is a constructor-injected val — declared as a class property with type. Regex catches it.
Where should this live? A new module `barrier_contract.py` containing: CanonicalBarrierContract, CANONICAL_BARRIER_CONTRACT_V1, receiver type resolution (`resolve_receiver_fqcn(masked_text, receiver_name, span...)`), and the proof engine? The plan says "a shared direct-barrier proof module" — I'll create `barrier_proof.py` with the contract + receiver resolution + dominance proof.
### 3. Dominance proof
Input: masked_text, body_span, mutation sites (with spans), barrier markers (collect_barrier_markers output), CFG (from build_callable_cfg with --include-graphs or direct build), contract, file-level type evidence.
For each mutation site:
- Find its MUTATION node in the CFG (node with kind MUTATION at the site's span start).
- Candidate barrier nodes: BARRIER_CALL nodes whose underlying call resolves to the canonical contract (receiver FQCN resolves to contract.receiver_fqcn AND method in contract.direct_check_methods) — resolution via markers: each BARRIER_CALL node corresponds to a DIRECT_CHECK region or an attached marker. I need receiver + method per barrier node. The CFG nodes only have spans. Barrier markers have method + span; regions have barrier kind + span. Approach: build a map span→(method, receiver_text) by scanning the masked text at barrier node spans: extract the call text within the node span, parse receiver simple name and method name. Then resolve receiver FQCN via the file evidence.
- Dominators: standard iterative algorithm on the CFG graph (nodes/edges as ids) from ENTRY, over the REACHABLE subgraph. A barrier node dominates mutation iff barrier ∈ dom(mutation).
- Also: the barrier must lie on the NORMAL path (the plan: "the barrier node lies on the normal-return path"). With the CFG, barrier nodes on normal flow already; exceptional-only barriers (in catch) — a barrier in catch is on the EXCEPTION edge path; it can still dominate a mutation inside the catch body — plan says "Barrier in catch only → COUNTEREXAMPLE unless every path to mutation reaches it". The dominance calculation handles this naturally: if mutation is in the catch body, entry→mutation paths go through the catch → barrier inside catch before mutation dominates → PROVEN. That matches "unless every path to mutation reaches it". Good — pure dominance is the correct semantic.
- UNKNOWN edges: "no unknown/unsupported edge can reach m without the barrier". Conservative treatment: compute dominance normally; then check: for each node NOT dominated by any canonical barrier that still reaches the mutation... that IS the dominance condition — if barrier dominates mutation, every path crosses it, including paths via UNKNOWN edges. So pure dominance covers it. But subtlety: UNKNOWN edges go to exit_normal mostly (BARRIER_SCOPE → UNKNOWN → exit). Fine.
- GUARDED_SCOPE: mutation inside a canonical runWrite lambda. GR-11's CFG: BARRIER_SCOPE region — is the mutation INSIDE the scope? In cfg.py, BARRIER_SCOPE builds children INSIDE: scope → children flow → last→UNKNOWN back to scope... wait: `for last in scope_lasts: builder.edge(EdgeKind.UNKNOWN, last, scope)` — children flow back INTO the scope node with UNKNOWN edges?! And return ([scope], [], [UNKNOWN, scope] + scope_dangling) — the scope's dangling goes to the join. So the scope node is a hub: entry edge in, children edges out (via scope_firsts — wait no: `return ([scope], [], ...)` — firsts = [scope]; the children's edges from scope? Look again: BARRIER_SCOPE handler: scope node added; _build_regions(children) gives child_firsts but `scope_firsts` is UNUSED (`_ = scope_firsts`); children lasts → UNKNOWN → scope; dangling → scope's dangling. So children are NOT connected from the scope node! The children subgraph is DISCONNECTED from entry (unreachable except... nothing points to child_firsts). So mutations inside a runWrite scope attach to nodes unreachable from ENTRY. Dominance from ENTRY would report those mutations as UNREACHABLE. How to treat? Plan section B: guarded scope proof requires mutation inside the exact lambda span + contract verifies + scope entry dominates. Since the scope model deliberately keeps lambda execution unproven (UNKNOWN), GR-12's direct proof for GUARDED_SCOPE-form mutations: UNSUPPORTED (per plan: runWrite lambda proof requires the contract to verify synchronicity — our contract DOES verify runWrite invokes block() exactly once after check! But GR-11's CFG doesn't wire scope children from the scope entry, so the CFG can't express it. Options: (a) treat unreachable mutation nodes inside a BARRIER_SCOPE whose receiver resolves canonically as PROVEN via lexical containment (plan B conditions — all verifiable: receiver canonical, lambda span containment, contract check-before-invoke verified by source lines 37-38, no edge from outside reaches mutation — true since children are only connected within the scope). Since zero production call sites use runWrite, this path is theoretical. I'll implement lexical guarded-scope proof: mutation inside a canonical BARRIER_SCOPE's lambda span → PROVEN (scope form), but conservative: if the scope's receiver doesn't resolve canonically → UNSUPPORTED.
Hmm — careful: "no modeled control-flow edge reaches the mutation from outside the guarded lambda scope" — since children are disconnected from entry, no entry path reaches them at all. But mutation sites attach via _attach_site: if the site span falls in a region... For a mutation inside a runWrite lambda: the site is inside the scope's children regions → attaches to innermost node inside the scope subgraph → unreachable from entry. So proof: unreachable + inside canonical scope span → PROVEN (guarded scope). If unreachable and NOT inside any canonical scope → INFRASTRUCTURE? That would be a broken model (site in no modeled region attaches to ENTRY — reachable). Unreachable mutation = inside disconnected scope subgraph → must be inside some BARRIER_SCOPE region → if receiver canonical → PROVEN; else → UNSUPPORTED (DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_PROOF_UNSUPPORTED).
- Counterexample reconstruction: when no canonical barrier dominates, find a concrete path ENTRY→mutation avoiding all canonical barrier nodes (BFS/DFS on the graph excluding barrier nodes) → node kinds + line numbers sequence (bounded).
- Multiple barriers: any canonical barrier dominating suffices? For DIRECT_CHECK semantics — the check must dominate. Any one canonical barrier that dominates the mutation → PROVEN. But also "barrier node lies on the normal-return path" — dominance over the reachable graph includes paths through exceptional edges; a barrier in catch can dominate a mutation in the catch body — correct per plan.
- Overload exactness: contract pins method names; same-name on other receiver → excluded by receiver resolution. "An overload not explicitly represented by the contract is not a barrier" — the contract lists direct_check_methods as names; arity/param resolution is beyond lexical scope — record in contract doc. The contract also has receiver FQCN exactness. Good enough per plan's typed contract (receiverFqcn + method names + version).
- Comments/strings: masked text → no markers ✓.
- Result fields per plan: callableKey, mutationKey, status (PROVEN/COUNTEREXAMPLE/UNSUPPORTED/INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE), proofVersion, barrierSite (node id/line), mutationSite, counterexampleNodeKinds, counterexampleLineSequence, diagnostics. Privacy: no raw source, bounded identifiers.

546	def _mutation_identity(site) -> str:
547	    getter = getattr(site, "callable_key", None)
548	    base = getter() if callable(getter) else (getter or "")
549	    return (
550	        base

552	        + getattr(site, "dao_accessor", "")
553	        + "|"
554	        + getattr(site, "dao_fqcn", "")
555	        + "|"

562	        if span_start <= offset < span_end:
563	            return "GUARDED_SCOPE"
564	    return None

def prove(source, sites_needles, contract=CANONICAL_BARRIER_CONTRACT_V1):
masked = mask_kotlin_source(source)

185	    def resolve(self, receiver_name: str) -> tuple[str | None, str]:
186	        declared = self._declarations.get(receiver_name)
187	        if not declared:
188	            return None, _RECEIVER_NOT_A_PROPERTY
189	        if len(set(declared)) > 1:
190	            return None, _RECEIVER_AMBIGUOUS
191	        simple = declared[0]
192	        if "." in simple:
193	            return simple, _RECEIVER_RESOLVED
194	        if simple in self._imports:
195	            candidates = self._imports[simple]
196	            if len(candidates) > 1:
197	                return None, _RECEIVER_AMBIGUOUS
198	            return next(iter(candidates)), _RECEIVER_RESOLVED
199	        if self._package:
200	            return self._package + "." + simple, _RECEIVER_RESOLVED
201	        return None, _RECEIVER_UNRESOLVED

270	        from scripts.db_guard.structural_analysis.tokenizer import (
271	            parse_callable_body,
272	        )
273	        from scripts.db_guard.structural_analysis.shadow_report import (
274	            _default_opacity_predicate,
275	        )
276
277	        # The parse must use the SAME opacity gate and the SAME site set the
278	        # structural shadow pipeline uses: ALL resolved mutations of the
279	        # callable (a lambda hiding any other row's mutation must never be
280	        # modeled opaque).
281	        callable_sites = tuple(
282	            MutationSite.from_observation(item)
283	            for item in sorted(
284	                obs_by_callable.get(observation.callable_key, ()),
285	                key=lambda item: (item.source_start, item.source_end),
286	            )
287	        )
288	        opacity = _default_opacity_predicate(masked, body_span, callable_sites)
289	        parse_result = parse_callable_body(
290	            masked, body_span, lambda_opacity_predicate=opacity
291	        )
292	        if parse_result.unsupported:
293	            after_rows.append(
294	                {
295	                    "mutationKey": mutation_key,
296	                    "callableKey": callable_key,
297	                    "path": entry.path,
298	                    "proofStatus": "UNSUPPORTED",
299	                    "diagnosticCode": "DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_PROOF_UNSUPPORTED",
300	                    "reason": "callable not conservatively modelable",
301	                }
302	            )
303	            continue
304	        mutation_site = next(
305	            item for item in callable_sites if item.span.start == observation.source_start
306	        )

37:     "        list.forEach { item ->\n"

45: def _policy_entry():
46:     return types.SimpleNamespace(
47:         path=_PROJECT,
48:         owner_fqcn="com.example.Repo",
49:         kind="function",
50:         method="write",
51:         receiver=None,
52:         parameter_types=("Int",),
53:     )
54:
55:
56: def _observation(source: str):
57:     call_start = source.index("dao.insert")
58:     return build_mutation_observation(
59:         path=_PROJECT,
60:         owner_fqcn="com.example.Repo",
61:         kind="function",
62:         method="write",
63:         receiver=None,
64:         parameter_types=("Int",),
65:         source=source,
66:         call_start=call_start,
67:         call_end=call_start + len("dao.insert(x)"),
68:         dao_accessor="dao",
69:         dao_fqcn="com.example.Dao",
70:         operation="insert",
71:         mutation_kind="ROOM_ABSTRACT_INSERT",
72:         source_identity="com.example.Dao::dao#insert",
73:     )
74:
75:
76: def _declaration(source: str):
77:     body_open = source.index("{", source.index("fun write"))
78:     depth = 0
79:     closing = None
80:     for offset in range(body_open, len(source)):
81:         if source[offset] == "{":
82:             depth += 1
83:         elif source[offset] == "}":
84:             depth -= 1
85:             if depth == 0:
86:                 closing = offset
87:                 break
88:     assert closing is not None
89:     # Declaration-scanner convention: the body span excludes BOTH braces
90:     # (body_start = after "{", body_end = index of "}").
91:     return DeclarationRange(
92:         path=_PROJECT,
93:         owner_fqcn="com.example.Repo",
94:         kind="function",
95:         start_line=source.count("\n", 0, body_open) + 1,
96:         end_line=source.count("\n", 0, closing) + 1,
97:         is_dao=False,
98:         is_abstract=False,
99:         body_start=body_open + 1,
100:         body_end=closing,
101:         callable_name="write",
102:         parameters=("Int",),
103:         source_start=source.index("fun write"),
104:         source_end=closing + 1,
105:     )
106:
107:
108: def _write_project(tmp_path, source: str):
109:     kotlin_file = tmp_path / _PROJECT
110:     kotlin_file.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
111:     kotlin_file.write_text(source, encoding="utf-8")
112:

Thinking
The test "row without proof result" — declaration index empty → `containing` != 1 → uncorrelated, but I don't append an after_row in the uncorrelated path! Looking at my CLI code: when `len(containing) != 1`, I do `uncorrelated.append(mutation_key); continue` — WITHOUT appending an after_row. So the after report has 0 entries for that row → summary directEntryCount 0, and the exit... after_summary counts zero unsupported → falls through to... failure_reasons empty, unsupported 0, counterexample 0 → exit 0! BAD — violates the plan's safety rule 6: "direct policy row missing proof result exits 2".
Fix: in the correlation-failure branch, append an after_row with INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE + DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_CONTRACT_INVALID (or UNSUPPORTED). Which? A row missing its proof result = infrastructure uncertainty → exit 2. Use proofStatus "INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE", diagnosticCode "DB_DIRECT_BARRIER_CONTRACT_INVALID" — matches plan's required treatment (direct policy row missing proof result exits 2).
Also the `identity is None` branch: same fix.
The CLI silently drops rows that fail correlation — violating the plan's "direct policy row missing proof result exits 2" rule. Fixing: correlation failures must encode an INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE row.
